There, the fishermen, known as Parathavars, weave fishing nets. Garlands of Serunthi flowers are woven by the Parathiya women, who have red, long eyes. Salt is measured there. There are salt pans. The fishermen living on the coast catch fish from the sea, kill them, and engage in the cruel profession. Here, Sekkizhar Peruman describes the fishing profession as a murderous and cruel profession with great anguish. The heart of Sekkizhar, the compassionate guru, trembles when he thinks of the fishing profession. Some meat-eaters say that fish is edible meat. Sheep, chickens, etc., are killed by human hands and are considered large meat. Therefore, only they are called meat, and fish is not considered meat because fish die on their own without being killed, and that fish food comes from them. Therefore, they argue that eating fish is not non-vegetarian. Some try to justify their improper actions in this way. Buddhists, who follow the path of Buddha, who preached "Oon oon thuramin uyir kolai neengumin" (Give up meat, avoid killing living beings), and even Brahmins in North and West India, hold this view and consume fish. Thirugnanasambandha Peruman has also condemned the lack of compassion among Buddhists regarding this. The act of separating aquatic creatures like fish from water, which is essential for their survival, is considered killing them. Just as air is essential for the survival of creatures like us living on land, water is an essential part of the life of aquatic creatures like fish. If a human is deprived of air, they will die. Is that murder? Yes, it is murder. Similarly, if fish are taken out of the sea and die, that is also murder. Therefore, Sekkizhar Peruman describes the fishing profession as a cruel profession. "Thozhil" here refers to a traditional occupation for livelihood. Why does Sekkizhar Peruman call it a cruel profession here? Because by separating fish from water and causing their death, and then consuming those fish, all those people will suffer in this life and go to hell in the next. Therefore, out of compassion, Sekkizhar Peruman sings that people should not engage in this cruel profession and go to hell. The profession of going into the sea and catching fish is the work of the Nuzhaiyar (fishermen). Selling them is the profession of Nuzhaichiyar (fisherwomen). Pearl diving, collecting corals, etc., are the professions of the men living in the Neythal land. Selling the pearls and corals they have collected is the profession of the fisherwomen. On the seashore, Punnai flowers bloom on Punnai trees. There are also Thazhai plants with long spathes. Thus, Sekkizhar Peruman, who describes the Neythal land in detail, now explains why the Neythal land in Thondai Nadu is special. It is special because it contains the Shiva temples located in the Neythal lands of Thondai Nadu. Sekkizhar Peruman sings about three holy places located in the Neythal land: Thiruvottriyur, Thirumylai (Mylapore), and Thiruvanmiyur. These three holy places are located on the seashore, in the Neythal land of Thondai Nadu. First, Sekkizhar Peruman sings about Thiruvottriyur. The special features of Thiruvottriyur are as follows. This Sthalam, Thiruvottriyur, is very famous. It is a well-known Sthalam. It is a Sthalam in Chennai. The Lord's names here are Adhipureeswarar, Putridam Kondar, Padambakkanathar, Ezhuthariyum Perumal, Thyagesar, Ananda Thyagesar. The Goddess's names are Thirupurasundari, Vadivudai Ammai, Vadivudai Manikkam. The Sthala Viruksham is the Magizha tree. The Theertham is Brahma Theertham. Those who worshipped here include Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, Appar Swamigal, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, Kapiladeva Nayanar, Paranadeva Nayanar, Pattinathu Pillaiyar, Nambiandar Nambigal, Sekkizhar Peruman, Aiyadigal Kadavarkon Nayanar, Musukunda Chakravarthi, Brahma, Thirumal, Nandidevar, Chandra, Valmiki Munivar, 27 Nakshatras, etc. The Moolasthanam (sanctum sanctorum) has a Gajaprushta (elephant-back) structure. The Moolavar is a Swayambhu Thirumeni (self-manifested idol). It is a Shiva Lingam in the form of a snake. The Shiva Lingam and the Avudaiyar (pedestal) are square-shaped. A square-shaped kavacham (armor) is placed on the Lord. Only once a year, on the full moon day of Karthigai month, is this kavacham removed, and only Punugu Sattam, Javvadhu, and Sambirani Thailam (fragrant pastes and oil) are applied. For the first three days from this day, the Lord remains without the kavacham. Then, the kavacham is reapplied, and the Lord is seen with the kavacham throughout the year. All abhishekams are performed only for the Avudaiyar. This is the holy place where Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's wife, Sangiliyar, resided. We will see in Periya Puranam that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal stayed here for a long time. Sekkizhar Peruman composes a song about Thiruvottriyur as follows: "Marutkodum thozhil mannavan irakkiya variyai nerikki mun Thiruvottriyur neenga endru ezhuthum oruthar thamperum koyilin orupuram soozhntha thirupparappaiyum udaiyathu aththiraik kadal varappu," meaning the vast expanse of the sea-coast, where the great temple of the one who wrote "Exclude Thiruvottriyur" before reducing the taxes imposed by the king who engaged in cruel deeds, is surrounded on one side. The story of the "one who engaged in cruel deeds" is found in the Sthala Puranam of Thiruvottriyur. In Kritha Yuga, a king named Mandatha ruled Ayodhya. He was one who did not acquire wealth through deceit, sought truth, ruled the world according to royal justice, adorned his body with sacred ash, was a devotee of Shiva, a servant of Shiva's devotees, and performed various Shiva dharmas. As time passed, this king grew old, aged, became gray, his flesh sagged, his teeth fell out, and his words faltered, causing him distress. At that time, he asked some deceitful people, "O people, if I am to be relieved of my body quickly without suffering like this, what should I do?" They gave him wrong advice, saying, "If you perform actions that diminish your accumulated merits, your life will end quickly, and your body will depart." Hearing these words, King Mandatha called his minister and commanded him, "Go immediately and reduce the accounts of all the charities I have been performing for all the Shiva temples." That is, he commanded his minister to reduce all the charities the king was providing to Shiva temples and holy places. So, the minister, who was an accountant, reduced all the expenses for Shiva dharmas related to Shiva temples in the account book. The next morning, when he looked at the book, he saw "Excluding Thiruvottriyur" written before the first line that was already written in the book. The king tried to punish the accountant. Then the accountant said, "That is not my handwriting. I did not write that the accounts of Shiva dharmas in all Shiva temples, excluding Thiruvottriyur, should be reduced." Then, seeing that the handwriting was different, the king's anger subsided. He took a new sheet of paper and wrote, "I am reducing the accounts of Shiva dharmas for Shiva temples." The next morning, "Excluding Thiruvottriyur" was again written on the first line of that sheet. Therefore, there was a royal decree that Shiva dharmas in all Shiva temples, excluding Thiruvottriyur, would be reduced. The astonished king, realizing the glory of the Lord of Thiruvottriyur, immediately went to Thiruvottriyur, bathed in Brahma Theertham, worshipped Padambakka Nathar, and regained his youth, just as before, his old age having vanished. In the first line of the eight lines where the accountant wrote to reduce Shiva dharma in all Shiva temples, the Lord Himself wrote, "Excluding Thiruvottriyur, reduce in all Shiva temples," splitting the first line. Therefore, the Lord was called Ezhuthariyum Peruman (the Lord who knows how to write). Sekkizhar Peruman has incorporated this Sthala Purana story into his sacred hymn, singing, "Marutkodum thozhil mannavan irakkiya variyai nerikki mun Thiruvottriyur neenga endru ezhuthum oruthar," referring to the one who wrote "Exclude Thiruvottriyur" before reducing the taxes imposed by the king who engaged in cruel deeds. Here, the Lord, having received Shiva Diksha from Upamanyu Mahamunivar and worshipped Vasuki, has incorporated Vasuki into His sacred form and resides with those marks even today. Therefore, the Lord is named Padambakka Nathar. This is the place where Sundaramoorthy Swamigal married Sangili Ammaiyar. It is a Sthalam sung by the three Moovar Mudhaligal (Appar, Sundarar, Sambandar). Thiruvottriyur, like Thiruvarur and Kanchipuram, is a Shiva-blessed holy place, as explained by the history of Aludaiya Nambigal. Among our 63 Nayanmars, Kaliya Nayanar was born and performed the service of lighting lamps in Thiruvottriyur. This is the place where the ghost sugarcane tasted sweet to Pattinathar, also known as Pattinathu Swamigal. Therefore, he decided this was his rightful place and attained Samadhi near the seashore. His temple is still there today. This Sthalam is praised by Pattinathu Adigal in the work "Thiruvottriyur Orupa Orupathu," compiled in the 11th Thirumurai. Vattaparai Amman resides here with great fame. Sri Rama's son, Lava, came here, worshipped the Lord, and performed Rajasuya Yagam. A king named Thondaiman performed renovation work. The Lord showed His wedding form to Agasthiyar here. Adiseshan and Chandra have worshipped here. Panini Munivar received Upadesam (spiritual instruction) from Lord Shiva here and composed Vyakarana (grammar) based on those Sutras. In this Sthalam, Lord Shiva established all the Shiva Ganas to guard, so this place is called Bhuloka Shivalokam (Shiva's abode on earth). It is named Adhipuri because it appeared at the beginning during the great deluge. It also has the names Shivapuri, Padmapuri, Avasapuri, Brahmapuri, Nirantharapuri. The glories of this Sthalam are mentioned in the Padma Purana and Brahmanda Purana in Sanskrit. Thyagaraja Peruman also resides in this Sthalam. Here, He is known as

He is known as Manikka Thyagar. The dance performed by Thyagaraja Peruman here is called Kundikkondaaduthal. "All ponds are sacred waters, all sand is sacred ash, all groves are Gananathar's, this is Shiva's abode on earth," thus sing the true ascetics about Thiruvottriyur, which is a very famous hymn about Thiruvottriyur. Thus, Sekkizhar Peruman has sung about Thiruvottriyur, one of the Shiva temples located in the coastal Neythal region of Thondai Nadu. We have briefly reflected on its Sthala Puranas. Sekkizhar Peruman also sings about Thirumylai and Thiruvanmiyur. We will continue to reflect. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 09 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periya Puranam, Part 217, Mummaiyal Ulaganda Sarukkam, Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. Sekkizhar, who sings about the Shiva holy places in the Neythal land (sea and its surrounding areas) in Thondai Nadu, first sings about Thiruvottriyur, then about Thirumylai, also known as Mylapore, and Thiruvanmiyur. Sekkizhar Peruman sings that the Neythal land contains Thirumylapuri, also known as Thirumylai, and Thiruvanmiyur, which are Shiva's abodes. Sekkizhar Peruman has composed the hymn as "Meitharum pugazh Thirumylapuri virai soozh moythayangu than pozhil Thiruvanmiyur mudhala," meaning "Thirumylapuri of true fame, and Thiruvanmiyur with its fragrant, dense, cool groves, and so on." First, Sekkizhar Peruman sings about Thirumylai, using the epithet "Meitharum pugazh Thirumylapuri" (Thirumylapuri of true fame) for Mylapore. If "Mei" means truth, this holy place proclaims and establishes the truth that the greatest benefit for those born on earth is the worship of the Lord and the veneration of His devotees. If "Mei" is taken as body, it also implies Mylapuri, which is famous for creating a body out of bones and then embedding life into it. This Mylapuri, also known as Mylapore, is the 24th Sthalam among the sung Sthalams in Thondai Nadu. It is also known as Mayil Aarppu, Mylappu, and Mylai. It is mentioned in Thevarams as "Mylappil ullar punnaiyan kaanan mada mylai." This name came about because the Goddess, in the form of a peacock, worshipped the Lord under a Punnai tree. The Punnai tree and the sacred idol of the Goddess in peacock form worshipping under it can still be seen in this temple. The Lord's name is Kapaleeswarar, and the Goddess's name is Karpagavalli Ammai. The Sthala Viruksham is the Punnai tree, and the Theerthams include Kapali Theertham. Those who worshipped the Lord here include Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, Appar Swamigal, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, Aiyadigal Kadavar Kon Nayanar, Nambiandar Nambi, Sekkizhar Peruman, and Sri Ramar. The temple's name is Kapaleeswaram. This is the place where Vayilar Nayanar, one of the 63 Nayanmars, was born and attained liberation. The Sthala Puranam of Mylapore is found in the Kotirudra Samhita of the Saiva Mahapurana in Sanskrit, specifically in the Utkrishta Sushetha Prakarana, consisting of 11 chapters titled Kapaleesa Sthala Mahatmyam. In Tamil, there are two Sthala Puranas. One is composed by Srimath Amirthalingam Thambiran Swamigal of the Mylai branch math of Kunrakkudi Adheenam. Another Sthala Puranam, titled Thirumylai Sthala Puranam, was recently written by Mylai Nathamuni Mudaliyar. The Kapaleeswaram temple faces west. It has a small western gopuram. At the entrance of this gopuram, on the north side is Karpaga Vinayagar, and on the south side is Bala Murugan. After worshipping Karpaga Vinayagar and Bala Murugan, when entering the temple through the western gopuram, the Angam Poombavai shrine is located. After passing the Angam Poombavai shrine in the western inner prakaram, and proceeding north to the eastern inner prakaram, the Punnai Vananathar shrine is located. The Sthala Viruksham, the Punnai tree, is located here. In the eastern inner prakaram, the Sundaresar shrine is located. The Navagrahas are located. The Jagadeeswarar shrine is located. The Koothadum Pillaiyar (Dancing Ganesha) shrine, named Narthana Vinayagar shrine, is located directly opposite the eastern gopuram (Rajagopuram entrance). To the south of the Narthana Vinayagar shrine, the Annamalaiyar shrine is located. In the southern prakaram, facing west, the Singaravelar shrine is located.

Here, Singaravelar is seen with Valli and Deivanai. In the southern prakaram, there is the Palaniandavar shrine, and then to the west of the Palaniandavar shrine, the Vayilar Nayanar shrine is located. Directly opposite the western gopuram, the idol of Nandidevar is placed facing Kapaleeswarar. The flagstaff and Balipeedam are next to the idol of Nandidevar. The Kapaleeswarar shrine is located inside. To the north of the Kapaleeswarar sanctum, facing south, is the Natarajar shrine. The idols of the sixty-three Nayanmars are located. Next to the processional idols of the sixty-three Nayanmars, the idols of Saraswati, Durga, and Lakshmi are facing east. In front of the Kapaleeswarar shrine, to the left, facing south, is the Karpagambal shrine. In front of this shrine, there is a lion idol and a Balipeedam. In the southwest corner of this shrine, the Palliyarai (bedchamber) is located. Now, let's briefly reflect on the Sthala Puranam of the temple. On Mount Kailash, when Lord Shiva was imparting the glory of Sri Panchaksharam to Goddess Parvati, a peacock spread its feathers and danced. Goddess Parvati, instead of listening to Lord Shiva's discourse, watched the peacock with great interest. Afterwards, she requested Lord Shiva to teach her again. At that time, Lord Shiva told Goddess Parvati, "Since you were watching the peacock, worship me in the form of a peacock." Accordingly, the Goddess, in the form of a peacock, worshipped Kapaleeswarar under a Punnai tree according to the Agamas. At that time, the Lord appeared to Goddess Parvati and blessed her. Then, the Goddess requested Lord Shiva that since she worshipped as a peacock, this place should be called Thirumylai by the elders, and all those who meditate on them here should receive all blessings and then attain liberation. The Lord granted her wish. Here, Meeneeswarar, worshipped by the Vedas, resides. The Mylai Sthala Puranam states that the story of Lord Shiva giving the spear to Lord Muruga, as found in Kandapuranam, took place here. Therefore, Lord Muruga is known as Singaravelar here. Lord Shiva is known as Kapaleeswarar here. Once, Lord Brahma had five heads. Therefore, he became arrogant, thinking himself equal to Lord Shiva. At that time, Lord Shiva plucked Brahma's fifth head, which was looking up at the sky, with His nail. Then, Brahma's arrogance vanished, and he praised the Lord. The Lord then graciously said that if Brahma worshipped Him in Thirumylai, He would bless him. Then, Lord Brahma came here to Mylapore, Thirumylai, and worshipped Kapaleeswarar. At that time, Lord Brahma requested Lord Shiva to grant him the task of creation, and that this place should be known as Kapali Nagar, the Lord as Kapaleeswarar, and the Shakti Ganga Theertham as Kapali Theertham. The Lord granted his wish. When Thirumal took the Vamana avatar, and Mahabali tried to give charity to Thirumal, Sukracharya, in the form of a beetle, blocked the spout of the water pot in Bali Chakravarthy's hand. At that time, Vamana pricked him with a darbha grass, and Sukracharya lost his eye. He came here to Thirumylai and prayed to Kapaleeswarar. By the grace of the Lord, Sukracharya regained his eyesight. The Theerthams in Thirumylai holy place are as follows. The Uttama Kapali Theertham, located to the west of the Kapaleeswarar temple, is also known as Shakti Ganga. This is the primary Theertham. Thirumylai Sea is called Kadal Kadavul Theertham. Seven Theerthams are located here: Veda Theertham, Vali Theertham, Ganga Theertham, Velli Theertham, Rama Theertham. In Ramayana, when Sri Rama was searching for Sita Devi, he worshipped Kapaleeswarar in Thirumylai, received blessings, went to Lanka, killed Ravana, rescued Sita Devi, and after ruling Ayodhya, he again came to Thirumylai, created a Theertham, and worshipped Kapaleeswarar by performing Brahmotsavam in Aippasi. That is called Aippasi Onam festival. Mylapore is one of the important Sthalams where festivals are held throughout the year. The Perunchottru festival is held in the month of Aippasi on the Onam star. The Vilakkedu festival is held in the month of Karthigai on the Karthigai star. The Thiruvathirai festival is held in the month of Margazhi. The Poosam festival is held in the month of Thai. The Kadalattru festival is held on Masi Magam day. The Panguni Uthiram festival, Ashtami day festival, Potrapu festival, Perunchanthi are all celebrated in Kapaleeswarar temple, as mentioned by Thirugnanasambandha Peruman in his Thiruppathigam. On the first day of Chithirai, which is the New Year, Mahabhishekam is performed for Natarajar at Uchi Kalam on Chithirai Thiruvonam. Vasantha festival for Singaravelar. In Vaikasi, Thirugnanasambandha Peruman festival is celebrated for 10 days. On Punarpoosam star, Sekkizhar Swamigal is placed on an elephant vehicle, and a street procession is held, followed by the traditional merging with the Natarajar shrine. In the month of Aani, on the Maham star, which is Manikkavasagar Peruman's birth star, a street procession is held in a flower palanquin at night. Then, the traditional festival of Manikkavasagar Peruman merging with Natarajar is held. On Aani Uthiram star, Aani Thirumanjanam is performed for Natarajar. On Aani Sadayam, Sangabhishekam is performed. On Fridays in Aadi month, special pujas are performed for Karpagavalli Ambal. On Aadi Pooram, sandal paste is applied to Karpagambal. At night, Ambigai goes on a street procession seated on a Gandharvi vehicle. The Panniru Thirumurai festival begins in this month and ends at the beginning of Avani month, lasting for 13 days. In Avani, on Vinayagar Chaturthi, a special festival is held for Narthana Vinayagar. Avani Moolam Pittu festival. On Avani Chaturdasi, Abhishekam is performed for Natarajar. On Purattasi Chaturdasi, Abhishekam is performed for Natarajar. Kolu during Navaratri. Onam festival in Aippasi. Skanda Sashti for Singaravelar. Vilakkedu festival in Karthigai month. 108 conch abhishekams for Kapaleeswarar on Somavarams in Karthigai. Dhanur Masa Puja for 30 days in Margazhi. Manikkavasagar Thiruvempavai festival for 10 days. Margazhi Thiruvathirai Natarajar Peruman Abhishekam Alangaram. On the first day of Thai month, Makar Sankranti, during Uttarayana Punyakalam, Chandrasekara Swami street procession. On the second day, Mattu Pongal, Karpagambal Kanni Utsavam. In the morning, Abhishekam, Alangaram, Aradhanai, and street procession for Amma on the tank bank. Thaipusam festival will also be held. Masi Kadalattru festival. Panguni month Brahmotsavam for 11 days. Followed by 10 days of Vidayattri Utsavam. Thus, Kapaleeswarar temple is filled with festivals throughout the year. These are all details about Sthala Puranas, temple structure, and festivals. Two different accounts are given about the ancient Kapaleeswarar temple in Mylapore. It is said that the current temple is a new temple built within 300 to 400 years, and the old temple is located on the seashore at the site of the current foreign religious worship place. There are sufficient evidences for both these accounts. About 500 years ago, Mylapore fell into the hands of the Portuguese. They demolished the temple and built a fort. Later, after some years, the temple was built at the present location, and so on, there are references. This is because Kapaleeswarar temple is sung by Thirugnanasambandha Peruman and Arunagirinathar Peruman in Thiruppugazh as being located on the seashore, with the sea waves embracing the temple. We will see the Puranam of Vayilar Nayanar, who was born, lived, and attained liberation in Mylapore, in that section of Periya Puranam. Similarly, we will see the sacred history of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman bringing Poombavai back to life in the history of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. Now, we must reflect on Thiruvanmiyur, another Sthalam located in the coastal Neythal land of Thondai Nadu. We will continue to reflect. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 10 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam Periyapuranam Part 218 Mummaiyaal Ulaganda Sarukkam Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. Sekkizhar Peruman, who sings about the sacred places located in the Neythal land of Thondai Nadu, has combined and sung about Mylapore and Thiruvanniyur in a single sacred verse. Thiruvanniyur is the 25th sacred place among the song-honored places of Thondai Nadu. Here too, Vanmeeganathar is enshrined facing west. Since the Devas churned the milk ocean and consecrated and worshipped a Shiva Lingam with the obtained Amrutham, the Lord here is also known by the name Marundeesar. The Moolalingam (main deity) of the Lord is a Swayambhu Lingam with streaks of white ash, enshrined by the name Paalvannanathar. The name of the Goddess here is Tripurasundari Sokkanayaki. The Sthala Vriksham (sacred tree) is the Vanni tree. The Theerthams (sacred waters) include Panchatheertham, Paapanaasini, etc. Those who worshipped here include Thirugnanasambandar Peruman, Appar Swamigal, Sekkizhar Peruman, Valmiki Muni, Agathiyar, Bringi Muni, Surya, the Vedas, Devas, Kamadhenu, and others. The Sthala Puranam states that five drops of water from Ganga fell from the head of Lord Shiva in five directions around the Thiruvanniyur temple, and these became the five Theerthams. These five Theerthams are Janmanasini, Kamanasini, Papanasini, Gnanadayini, and Mokshadayini. Besides these, Surya Theertham, Brahma Theertham, and Yama Theertham are also located here. A beautiful Thyagarajar shrine is located in the Thiruvanniyur temple. The Thiruvanniyur Sthala Puranam is in Sanskrit, and Sri Ashtavadanam Poovai Kalyanasundara Mudaliar has translated it into Tamil and composed it. This Sthala Puranam consists of 19 chapters and 1022 viruthams (verses). Valmiki Muni, who composed the Ramayana, came here and worshipped Lord Shiva as per the advice of Markandeya Muni. Lord Shiva showed him his dance form. Therefore, the Lord is known by the name Vanmeeganathar. Rakshasas worshipped the Lord here. Parthasarathy Peruman worshipped the Lord here. Surya worshipped here. Chandra worshipped here. Indra worshipped here. The Vedas worshipped the Lord here. Kamadhenu worshipped here and received blessings. Sri Rama worshipped here. On the fifth day of the Panguni festival, the event where Arulmigu Thyagarajar blessed Rama is celebrated as the fifth day procession. During the divine marriage of Parvati and Parameswara, when the North tilted and the South rose, Agathiya Mamuni, to balance it, came towards the South and worshipped the Lord in Thiruvanniyur. At that time, Lord Shiva taught Agathiyar about the types of naturally occurring medicinal plants in this world, their properties, which medicine is for which disease, and the greatness of medicines. Lord Shiva taught Agathiyar all these things. Humans have bodies according to the deeds they have done. The diseases that occur to them are 4448, and as a solution to these diseases, four types of plants – trees, shrubs, creepers, and grass – and their parts such as bark, root, leaf, fruit, seed, wood, and juice, and six tastes – astringent, pungent, bitter, sour, salty, and sweet – have been classified. Medicines can be made by combining these. A physician must possess honesty, purity of mind, compassion, good conduct, devotion to God, masculinity, and other qualities. A physician should be able to understand the condition of the pulse of the patients who come to them and diagnose the nature of the disease through the eight types of examinations mentioned in the texts. The physician should examine the patient's body condition, their diet, their age, their sleep, and then treat the patient for Vatham, Pitham, and Silettumam (three humors). The physician should speak kindly to the patient and provide appropriate medicines. These are the qualities of a good physician, Lord Shiva teaches Agastya about medicines here. Furthermore, diseases can be cured using three methods: Mani (gems), Mantram (incantations), and Aushadham (medicine). Mani refers to the root essence. Through the methods derived from it, one can travel anywhere. It helps in acquiring wealth like mountains and the eight great siddhis. 63 sub-essences like mercury are secondary to this root essence. These are not suitable for pills. They are suitable for medicines. Only sages know their properties and greatness. It is difficult for ordinary physicians to know them, Lord Shiva teaches Agastya Peruman all these things. Among mantras, the Panchaksharam, which includes Pati (Lord), Pasu (soul), and Pasam (bondage), is the most supreme. Seven crores of great mantras originated from it. If one thinks of or sees those who have thoroughly chanted this five-letter mantra, all troubles will disappear like dew before the sun. Earlier, when the milk ocean was churned, the nectar that appeared spilled on the shores. As a result, nine types of herbs sprouted. Medicines can be made from herbs and plants. We are giving you complete knowledge about these. You will discover new herbs with your intelligence, Lord Shiva teaches Agastya and bestows complete knowledge of medicines upon Agastya Muni in Thiruvanniyur. Therefore, Agastya Muni bowed down to Lord Shiva and prayed, "Oh Lord, you who have taught the world about the types of medicines through me, should be worshipped here by the name Marundeesar." Agastya Mamuni made this request to Lord Shiva. Furthermore, Agastya Muni requested the Lord that those who come to this place and worship you, Marundeesar, with sincere love, should live without disease. And the diseases they had should also disappear. Lord Shiva granted his wish. He also showed Agastya Muni his wedding form. Therefore, the Lord of Thiruvanniyur is known by the names Marundeesar and Aushadheeswarar. We all know the history of Bringi Muni. Bringi Muni would perform penance on Bringi Malai, located near Thiruvanniyur. In the evening, he would worship at the Thiruvanniyur temple. On the fourth day morning of the Panguni festival, during the Chandrasekarar Vimana festival, the event where the Lord showed Bringi Muni the right path is celebrated as a traditional festival. We say that Bringi Muni performed penance on the mountain near Thiruvanniyur, which was called Bringi Malai. That is now known as Parangi Malai. All these are found in the Thiruvanniyur Sthala Puranam. Now, we all know about Srimath Appayya Dikshitar very well. Chinnabomman, a king who ruled in Vellore, performed Kanakabhishekam for Appayya Dikshitar. The Vijayanagara kings and the Thanjavur Nayak kings supported Srimath Appayya Dikshitar. Appayya Dikshitar, a great scholar, stayed in a place called Vedachireni near Thiruvanniyur and performed yagnas. Every day, he walked from Vedachireni to Thiruvanniyur and worshipped Marundeeswarar. The place where Srimath Appayya Dikshitar stayed and performed yagnas daily, Vedachireni, is now known as Velachery. This is hearsay. Now, information about the temple festivals. Generally, in temples located along the coast, festivals are traditionally held based on the full moon day. It is very rare to hold festivals based on star calculations in such coastal temples. Thiruvanniyur, being a coastal temple, the festivals for Marundeesar and Thyagar are held on the full moon day according to Agamic rules, even though they should be held according to star calculations. During the Panguni festival, the full moon is considered the tenth day, and the Dhvajarohanam (flag hoisting) is performed accordingly. The Brahmotsavam in Panguni is a grand festival. On the seventh day, the chariot festival, on the ninth day, the Vanni tree service, on the tenth day, the divine dance performed by the Lord for Valmiki Muni, and on the eleventh day, the float festival take place. In the months of Chithirai, Vaikasi, Aani, and Aadi, the Nallvar Thirunakshatram and Adipatta Nayanar Utsavam are celebrated. On Aadi Pooram, Tripurasundari Ambal comes in procession on a Kamadhenu vehicle. In Avani, Vinayagar Chaturthi, in Purattasi, Navaratri, on Aippasi Pournami, Annabhishekam for Marundeeswarar, Kandashashti, Karthigai Deepam, and Arudra Darisanam are all celebrated grandly. On Karthigai Somavaram, 108 Sangabhishekam is performed. These are all the special features and Sthala Purana stories of Thiruvanniyur. Sekkizhar Peruman, who has thus sung about the Neythal land, now sings about the Thinaimayakkam (mixing of landscapes) in the Puranam of Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar. First, Neythal and Kurinji are mixed. Thinaimayakkam means regions where two types of landscapes are intertwined. For example, Kurinji is a mountain and its surrounding areas, and Neythal is the sea and its surrounding areas. When Neythal and Kurinji are mixed in Thinaimayakkam, it means places where mountains and their surrounding areas, and the sea and its surrounding areas, are intertwined. It is our tradition to call such places Thinaimayakkam. Sekkizhar Peruman, who described the five Thinais – Kurinji, Paalai, Mullai, Marutham, and Neythal – and their respective Mudal Porul (primary elements) and Karupporul (secondary elements), and the glories of the Shiva temples located in them, now begins to describe the richness of the regions where these five Thinais are intertwined. In Thinaimayakkam, "mayanguthal" means to join or mix. Scholars say that there are six types of this. Kurinji mixes separately with Neythal, Marutham, and Mullai, making three. Then Mullai mixes separately with Neythal and Marutham, making two. Then Marutham mixes with Neythal, making one, totaling six types. First, Sekkizhar Peruman sings about the Thinaimayakkam where Neythal and Kurinji are combined. In Thondai Nadu, the places where Kurinji (mountainous region) and Neythal (coastal region) are combined are called Kadal Mallapuram, which is known as Mamallapuram. It is also known as Kadal Mallai, Mamallapuram, and Mahabalipuram. Vaishnavites also consider Mahabalipuram as one of their sacred places. Mahabalipuram is one of the 108 Divya Desams praised by the Alvars. In Mahabalipuram, Lord Shiva is enshrined in a sanctum made of sandalwood. The temples here and the five chariots belonging to the Shiva temple were carved out of individual rocks on the hillside. Ordinary people refer to these five chariots as Panchapandavar Ther. Since it is a mountain on the coast, Sekkizhar Peruman sings about it as a land where Neythal and Kurinji are intertwined. Next, Sekkizhar Peruman sings about the land where Marutham and Kurinji are combined, and its richness. Next, he sings about the richness of the regions where Mullai and Kurinji are combined, then the glories and richness of the regions where Neythal and Mullai are combined, then the glories of the land where Marutham and Mullai are combined, and then the richness of the regions where Neythal and Marutham are combined. Thus, Sekkizhar Peruman sings about the glories, richness, and greatness of Thondai Nadu. From the 1083rd verse to the 1124th verse, Sekkizhar Peruman, with his divine poetic skill, describes the four lands and five Thinaigal of Thondai Nadu in great detail, showing the boundaries of Tamil grammar and following the path of righteousness. Madhava Sivagnana Yogigal, in his Kanchipuranam, in the Thirunattu Padalam, praises Sekkizhar Peruman's divine poetic quality, saying, "Who else but the poet of Kundrai, surrounded by Madhava Sivagnana Yogigal, can describe the richness of the four lands and five Thinaigal of Thondai Nadu, and how can devotees like us understand and describe it?" Thus, Sekkizhar Peruman, who sang about the richness of Thondai Nadu in the Puranam of Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar, now begins to sing about the glories of Kanchipuram, especially as a city. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 11 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam Periyapuranam Part 219 Mummaiyaal Ulaganda Sarukkam Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. Sekkizhar Peruman, who sang about all the glories of Thondai Nadu, now sings in great detail about the glories of Kanchipuram and the greatness of the Goddess's penance there. Sivakavimani Ayya mentions the following as the special features of Kanchipuram: The Mother, who is the mother of all worlds, worshipped the Lord in Kanchipuram, following the Agamic path, which she received from the Lord, for the salvation of the world. She came from Mount Kailash to Kanchipuram, found and worshipped a Shiva Lingam that miraculously sprouted under a mango tree on the banks of the Kampa river, and demonstrated Shiva Agamic worship for all living beings in the world. Afterwards, she is enshrined in Thirukamakottam, nurturing righteousness and protecting all living beings. Such is the glorious place, Kanchipuram. When the Mother was worshipping in Kanchipuram, the Kampa river swelled and overflowed. Because the Mother, trembling, embraced the Lord, the temple here came to be known as Ekambaram. "Kampam" means trembling. As the Mother embraced the Lord tightly with her bangled hands, the Lord's body softened, showing the marks of her bangles and breast, and he is enshrined here as Thazhuvak Kuzhaintha Nathar (the Lord who softened when embraced). Ekambaram is also the name given to this temple. Ekambaram can be separated as "Ekam Amram," where "Ekam" means one and "Amram" means mango tree, thus meaning "the one with a single mango tree." This name is given because the Lord is seated under a mango tree. Kanchipuram is the foremost among the seven places of liberation (Mukti Sthalams). Kanchipuram is the first of the five elemental shrines (Pancha Bhoota Sthalams), representing the element of Earth (Prithvi). In Kanchipuram, there are countless temples such as Thirukamakottam, Thirukumarakkottam, Kachchapesam, etc. In Thiruvekambam, Vikata Chakra Vinayagar is prominently enshrined in front of the thousand-pillared hall at the southern entrance. Here, the Lord's name is Thiru Ekambar, and the Goddess's name is Elavaar Kuzhali Kamakshi Ambal. The Sthala Vinayagar is Vikata Chakra Vinayagar, Murugar is Mavadi Kumara Nayagar, the Sthala Vriksham is the mango tree, and the Theerthams are Sivagangai, Kampa Nadi, etc. The elephant, rescued from the grip of a crocodile, became a mountain called Athigiri, on which Lord Vishnu is enshrined as Varadarajar in Kanchipuram. Kanchipuram is the place where Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who lost both his eyes due to breaking a vow in Thiruvottiyur, was granted his left eye. Kanchipuram is the place where our Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar attained the feet of the Lord whom he served. Kanchipuram is also the place where Sakkiya Nayanar and Aiyadigal Kadavarkon Nayanar received blessings. The Linga Murtis named Vellai Kambam, Kallak Kambam, and Nallak Kambam, worshipped by Brahma, Vishnu, and Rudra respectively, are enshrined for darshan in Ekambaram. The Thalam called Thirukachi Mayanam, where Lord Shiva performed a sacrifice using the Devas as fuel during the time of deluge, is located inside Thiruvekambam, in front of the flagstaff. Thirukachi Mayanam is a Thevara Vaippu Thalam (a place mentioned in Thevaram hymns). Thirukachi Metrali, where Vishnu, who performed penance to attain Shiva Sarupam, melted upon hearing Thirugnanasambandar Peruman's hymn and became a Shiva Lingam; Thiruvonakanthan Thali, worshipped by the Asuras Onan and Kanthan; Thirukachi Anekathangavatham, worshipped by Vinayagar, Kubera, etc.; and Thirukachi Nerikaraikkadu, worshipped by Indra, Budhan, etc. - these are the four Thevaram song-honored places located within the city limits of Kanchipuram. Among the 32 Thevaram song-honored places in Thondai Nadu, five are located within the city limits of Kanchipuram. The place where Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar received blessings is now called Mukteeswarar Temple. These are all special notes about the place written by Sivakavimani Ayya. Now, according to our tradition, we must contemplate the glories and special features of Kanchipuram. The Sthala Puranam of Kanchipuram is the Kanchipuranam, composed by Sivagnana Yogigal of Thirukailaya Paramparai Thiruvavaduthurai Adheenam, who is the author of Dravida Mahabhashyam. Kanchipuranam is a very extensive Sthala Puranam, encompassing Vedic concepts, Upanishadic concepts, Agamic concepts, and Thirumurai concepts. In Kanchipuranam, Sivagnana Yogigal has described all the Shiva temples located in Kanchipuram and their glories. Kanchipuranam is structured as Suta Mahamuni narrating the greatness of Kanchipuram to other munis in Naimisaranyam. Suta Mamuni teaches the other munis. The greatness of Kanchipuram is found in the Theertha Manmiyam of the Kalika Khanda, in the Sanatkumara Samhita, which is one of the six Samhitas in the Skanda Mahapurana, one of the 18 Puranas composed by Vyasa Muni, an incarnation of Vishnu. Suta Mahamuni states that Sanatkumara Muni heard this greatness of Kanchipuram from Thirunandi Devar, and Sanatkumara Muni taught it to Vyasa Muni, who then taught it to him. Once, on Mount Kailash, Parvati Devi, the Mother of the World, worshipped the feet of Lord Shiva and asked the following: "Oh Lord, the bliss of liberation is obtained by the darshan of your sacred feet. Only wise sages who have researched and understood the essence of the Vedas, and who have matured through penance, meditation, and samadhi, can attain the darshan of your sacred feet. It is difficult for others to attain the darshan of your sacred feet. So, how will other ignorant beings, animals, birds, and other living beings in this world attain the bliss of liberation by seeing you?" Parvati Devi, the Mother of the World, asked this question out of great compassion for living beings and made this request to Lord Shiva. Hearing this, Lord Shiva, filled with compassion for all living beings to survive and prosper, spoke with his divine mouth: "Oh Uma, we reside in a state of transcendence. We are pervading everywhere in the world. But no one will know us in that form. We will live in the supreme places of unchanging glory, visible to all. In all the supreme places on this earth, you and I will live, visible to all," the Lord spoke with his divine mouth. Hearing this, Uma Devi asked the Lord which those places were. Lord Shiva then listed the places where he lives with Uma Devi: Sethu, Madurai, Thiruchirappalli, Thiruvazhuvur, Thiruvaiyaru, Thiruvaidaimarudur, Thillai, Virudhachalam, Annamalai, Thiruvinjai, Thiruvothur, Kanchi, Thiruvalangadu, Kalahasti, Srisailam, Sithavadam, Virupaksham, Thriyambakam, Thiru Gokarnam, Ujjayini, Mahakalam, Kasi, Kedaram, Prabhasam, Himalayas, Mandaram, Kailash, and countless other places where we reside, the Lord said. Hearing this, Parvati Devi asked, "Oh Lord, which is the best among all these places?" The Lord replied that the holy city of Kanchi is the best among all places. He said that even thinking of that city of Kanchi, or hearing its name spoken by others, or speaking it oneself, or bowing towards its direction, will grant everyone the supreme bliss of liberation. The Lord told Parvati Devi that he and she would live happily in that place forever. Kanchipuram is superior to Kasi, and just as he resides in Kasi by the name Viswanathan, in the city of Kanchi, under a mango tree, he performs the five divine functions of creation, preservation, destruction, concealment, and grace, and resides as a Lingam by the name Ekambanathan, the Lord told the Goddess. If one comes to Kanchipuram and stays there for at least one day, one can attain liberation. Even if one passes through Kanchipuram on a journey to other places, or stays there for a moment out of desire for wealth, they will attain liberation. It is true that if one utters "Kanchi" even once by its very nature, all five great sins will be removed, and one will attain the supreme state. The Lord said that he created the city of Kanchi to destroy Kali. Furthermore, in that ancient city of Kanchi, seven rivers flow: Pambai Nadi, Kampai Nadi, Punniya Nadi, Manjal Nadi, Vegavathi, and Palaru. Lord Shiva teaches Parvati Devi the glories of the city of Kanchi, saying that even if one worships or thinks of such a glorious Kanchipuram from afar, all their sins will be removed, and they will attain the bliss of liberation. Thus, Madhava Sivagnana Yogigal, in Kanchipuranam, describes the glories of countless Shiva Sthalams within Kanchipuram. Kanchipuram is a city where one can see Shiva Sthalams and manifestations of Lord Shiva's grace everywhere. Even today, the city of Kanchi is known for its countless Shiva Sthalams. Among them, 108 Shiva Sthalams are very special. In Kanchipuranam, Madhava Sivagnana Swamigal has composed separate chapters about the following Shiva Sthalams. First, the Sthalam called Thirukachi Neri Karaikkadu. The Lord's name is Satyavratheswarar, Satyanatheswarar. The Utsava Ambal (processional deity) here is Brahmarambikai. The Sthala Vriksham is the Karaichetti (a type of shrub), and the Theertham is Indra Theertham. Indra and Budhan have worshipped here. This is located in Thirukalaimedu, slightly away from Kanchipuram. The Lord's name here is Satyavratheswarar. Therefore, Kanchipuram got the name Satyavrata Kshetram after this name. This is a Sthalam praised in the hymns of Thirugnanasambandar Peruman. Next, Punniyakotteesam. The Lord's name is Punniyakotteeswarar. Those who worshipped here are Thirumal and Gajendran, the elephant. If one bathes in the Theertham here and worships the Lord, the merit obtained will multiply a crore times. Therefore, it is Punniyakotteesam. Thirumal worshipped the Lord in the form of a cloud. Thirumal rescued the elephant Gajendran from the grip of a crocodile. With that elephant, Mahavishnu came to Kanchi and worshipped the Lord. The Lord appeared before Thirumal, was pleased, and granted him boons. Because of those boons, Thirumal is known here as Varathan, and the place where he resided, named after the elephant Gajendran, is called Athigiri, and it is glorified. Next, Sivasthanam. The Lord's name here is Brahmapureeswarar. It is a Sthalam worshipped by Brahma Devan. Next, Manikandeesam. The Lord's name here is Manikandeeswarar. It is a Sthalam worshipped by all Devas, including Thirumal. Next, Santhasayam. Vyasa Santhaleeswarar Temple. The Lord's name here is Santhaleeswarar. The worshipper was Vyasa Muni. Next, Angirasam. This is the first of the Sapthasthana Sthalams. The Lord's name here is Angirareesar. Those who worshipped here are Angiras, Athri, Kashyapar, Kuchchar, Gautamar, Vasishtar, Bhrigu, etc. Similarly, there are Sapthasthana Sthalams in Kanchi worshipped by the seven Rishis. Next, Athreesam, Kuchchesam. These are the second and third of the Sapthasthana Sthalams. They were consecrated and worshipped by Athri Muni and Kuchcha Muni.

Athreesam and Kuchchesam are their names. Therefore, two Shiva Lingams are located here. Next, Kashyapesam. The Lord's name is Kashyapesar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Kashyapa Muni. Next, Gautamesam. The Lord's name is Gautameswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Gautama Muni. This is the seventh of the Sapthasthana Sthalams. Next, Bhargavesam. This is the sixth of the Sapthasthana Sthalams. The Lord's name is Bhargaveswarar, Bhargeeswarar. The worshipper was Bhrigu Muni. The fifth of the Sapthasthana Sthalams is Vasishtesam. The Lord's name is Vasishteswarar. The worshipper was Vasishta Muni. Thus, there are Sapthasthana Sthalams in Kanchi worshipped by the seven Rishis. Next, Paracharesam, Parachareswarar Temple. The Lord's name is Parachareswarar. The worshipper was Parachara. Next, Adipeedam. The Lord's name is Adipeedesar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Thirumal. When Saraswati Devi came in the form of a river to destroy the sacrifice performed by Brahma Devar in Kanchi, Thirumal, by the command of Lord Shiva, tried to stop her. At that time, the river approached the city of Kanchi in the dark of night. Then Thirumal, to see the arrival of the river in the dark, stood as light, consecrated and worshipped a Shiva Lingam by the name Adipeedesam, received boons, and stopped the river Saraswati, thus protecting Brahma's sacrifice. Next, Garudesam. The Lord's name here is Muktheeswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Garuda. It is the Sthalam where Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar attained liberation. Garuda worshipped Lord Shiva here and received the boon to destroy the snakes, the sons of his enemy. Next, Panatharesam. The Lord's name is Panatharesar, Panamoodisar. Snakes have worshipped here. Seeing their lineage being destroyed by the boon Garuda received, the snakes came to Kanchi, consecrated and worshipped Panatharesar here. The Lord, pleased with the worship of the snakes, showed compassion and adorned his body with the snakes as ornaments. Next, Kayarohanam. The Lord's name is Kayarohaneswarar. Guru (Brihaspati) and Mahalakshmi have worshipped here. Next, Siddhisam. The Lord's name is Siddhisar. The Theertham is Siddha Theertham. The worshippers were Siddhas. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 12 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam Periyapuranam Part 220 Mummaiyaal Ulaganda Sarukkam Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. We are looking at the glories of Kanchi city according to Kanchipuranam. The next temple is Arisaba Bhayam Theertha Sthanam. The Lord's name is Arisaba Bhayam Theertha Eswarar. Next is Ishta Siddhisam. The Lord's name is Ishta Siddhiswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Dadhichi Muni. Next is Kachchapesam. The Lord's name is Kachchapeswarar. Those who worshipped here are Thirumal, Vinayagar, Aiyanar, Durga, Surya, and Bhairavar. Since Thirumal worshipped in the form of a tortoise, it is called Kachchapesam, and the Lord is also called Kachchapeswarar. Next is Surakesam. The Lord's name is Jurahareswarar. The Theertham is Jurahara Theertham. The Lord who cures fever and bodily heat is enshrined here by the name Jurahareswarar. Next is Thanthondreesam. The Lord's name is Upamanneeswarar. The worshipper was Upamanyu Mahamuni. Next is Amareswaram. The Lord's name is Amareswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by the Devas. Next is Thirukachi Anekathangavatham. The Lord's name is Anekathangavatheeswarar. The Sthala Vriksham is the mango tree. The Theertham is Vishnu Theertham. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Kubera and Vinayagar. It is a Sthalam praised in the hymns of Thirugnanasambandar Peruman and Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. Anekatham means elephant. It is a Sthalam worshipped by Vinayagar, who has an elephant face. Since there is a Sthalam by the name Anekathangavatham in the Himalayas, to differentiate it, the name of Kanchipuram, Kachi, is added in front, and this Sthalam is called Kachi Anekathangavatham. Next is Kailasanathar Temple. The Lord's name is Kailasanathar. Thirumal and Naradar have worshipped here. This is the very famous Kanchipuram Kailasanathar Temple. It was built by Rajasimha Pallava. It is a temple associated with the history of Poosalar Nayanar. Next are Veeraraghavesam and Lakshmanesam. The Lord's name here is Veeraragheswarar. The worshippers were Sri Rama and Lakshmana. Next is Balabhadra Ramesam. The Lord's name is Balabhadra Rameswarar. The worshipper was Balarama. Next is Thirukachi Metrali, Thirumetraliswarar Temple. It is located in Pillaiyarpalayam in Kanchipuram. The Lord's name is Thirumetraliswarar, Thirumetralinathar. The Sthala Vriksham is the mango tree. The Theertham is Vishnu Theertham. This Sthalam is praised in the hymns of Thirunavukkarasu Swamigal, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, and Sekkizhar Peruman. Next is Vanmeeganathesam. The Lord's name is Vanmeeganathar. The worshipper was Indra. Next is Vaivaravesam. The Lord's name is Soliswarar. Next is Thakkisam. The Lord's name is Thakkiswarar. The worshippers were Dakshan and all the Devas. Next is Thrippurari Kottam. It is located in the Iyya Pillaiyar Koil Mandapam area. The Lord's name is Thrippurariswarar. The worshippers were Sudhanman, Susilan, and Subathi. Next is Hiranyesam. The Lord's name is Hiranyeswarar. The worshipper was Hiranyan. Next is Narasimhesam. The Lord's name is Narasimheswarar. The worshipper was Narasimha Murthy. Next is Anthakesam. The Lord's name is Manikandeeswarar. The Goddess's name is Thripurasundari. The worshipper was Anthakasuran. Thirupputkuzhi is the name of this village, which is now called Manikandeeswarar Temple. Next is Vanesam. The Lord's name is Vaneswarar. The worshipper was Banasuran. Next is Onakanthan Thali. The Lord's name is Oneswarar, Kantheswarar. The Theertham is Vishnu Theertham. The worshippers were two Asuras named Onan and Kanthan, who were the commanders of Banasuran's army. This is also a Sthalam worshipped by Jalandharan. It is a Sthalam where Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's Thevaram hymns are found. Since Onan and Kanthan worshipped here, it is called Onakanthan Thali. Next is Thirumalperu, also called Thirumalpur. The Lord's name is Manikandeeswarar, Dhayanidheeswarar, Pravaleeswarar. The Goddess's name is Anjanakshi, Karunambikai. We have already discussed the glory of this Sthalam in detail when Sekkizhar Peruman sings about the glory of Thondai Nadu. Now, Parashurameswaram. The Lord's name is Parashurameswarar. The worshipper was Parashurama. Next is Renukeswaram. The Lord's name is Renukeswarar. The worshipper was Renuka Devi. Next is Laguleesam, also called Thavaleeswaram. The Lord's name is Laguleesar, Thavaleeswarar. The worshipper was Lagulisan. The history is that Yogacharyas, who appeared as Rudramsa, starting from Swethan, Swethaketu, Sudhakaran, Swethalohithan, Swethaseegan, Swethasvan, Dhundhubhi, and ending with Lagulisan, performed penance in Kailash. By the grace of the Lord, they consecrated and worshipped Shiva Lingams in Kanchi by their names. Except for one or two of these Shiva Lingams, due to our lack of penance, we have not been able to find the others. It is said that the above-mentioned Shiva Lingams were spread from Pallur to Sarva Theertham. Next is Sarva Theertham Ekambaranathar Temple. The Lord's name is Ekambaranathar. This is a sacred tank. Madhava Sivagnana Yogigal has dedicated a separate chapter called Sarva Theertha Padalam in Kanchipuranam to its glory. Next is Piravaathanam. The Lord's name is Piravaathanathiswarar, Piravatheeswarar. The worshipper was Vamadevar. Next is Iravaasthanam. The Lord's name is Iravaatheeswarar. The worshippers were Markandeyar and Swethan. Next is Mahalingesam. The Lord's name is Mahalingeswarar. The worshippers were Brahma and Thirumal. Next is Veerattakasam. The Lord's name is Veerattakasar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Thirumal, Siddhas, and Kongana Muni. Next is Pandavesam. The Lord's name is Pandaveswarar. The worshippers were the Pandavas. Next is Machchesam. The Lord's name is Machcheswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Thirumal in his Matsya avatar. Next is Abiramesam. The Lord's name is Abirameswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Vishnu. Next is Kannesam. The Lord's name is Kannesar. The worshipper was Thirumal. When the poison that arose from the milk ocean attacked Vishnu's body, Thirumal, who turned black, came to Kanchi, consecrated and worshipped Kannalingam. Therefore, it is called Kannesam. At the end of the worship, the Lord appeared before Thirumal and blessed him, saying, "You will be relieved of this heat by staying near the moon on my head, opposite my shrine in Thiruvekambam, Kanchipuram." Thirumal did so and was relieved of the heat, and is still known as Nilathunda Perumal, residing opposite the Ekambareswarar shrine in Kanchipuram. It is noteworthy that Kachi Nilathunda Perumal is a Perumal praised by the Alvars. Next is Kumarakottam, where Murugapperuman is enshrined. The Lord's name is Subramaniyar. He is the Murugapperuman worshipped by Kachiyappa Sivachariyar. This temple is also mentioned in Kanchipuranam. This is the place where Kandapuranam originated and was presented. The Moolavar (main deity) Murugapperuman is seen holding a rosary and a kamandalam, in the form of the creator. When Brahma did not know the meaning of Pranava, Murugapperuman imprisoned him and took over his work of creation, and this divine form is shown here. Next is Masathan Thali. The Lord's name here is Masathan Thaliswarar. The worshipper was Saththanar. Mahasaththan, also known as Mahasasta, appeared when the Lord was enchanted by Vishnu's Mohini form. He came to Kanchi, consecrated and worshipped a Shiva Lingam. That Sthalam is called Mahasasta Thali, also known as Masathan Thali. Next is Anantha Padmanabesar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Vishnu. Once, Lord and Goddess in Kailash decided to play a game. They appointed Vishnu as the referee for the game and continued playing. At the end of the game, the Goddess won. But the Lord claimed that he had won. Vishnu, as the referee, seeing the situation where both the Lord and Goddess claimed victory, wavered in his impartiality and declared that the Lord had won. The Goddess, who had actually won, became angry and cursed Thirumal. Thirumal, realizing his mistake, pleaded, and the compassionate Goddess told him to go to Kanchi, consecrate a Shiva Lingam, and worship it. Thirumal did so, came to this Sthalam, consecrated and worshipped Lord Shiva by the name Anantha Padmanaban, and was relieved of the curse. Next is Kachi Mayanam. The Lord's name is Mayana Lingam.

Eswarar. This is a Thevara Vaippu Sthalam. Once, Bandasuran entered the bodies of Brahma, Thirumal, and all other Devas, stole their vitality, and made them weak. To subdue Bandasuran, the Lord appeared as a pillar of light in Kanchi, created a sacrificial pit, filled it with ghee, used all principles as darbha grass, and the three gunas as sacrificial altars, and performed a sacrifice with the Goddess. In this sacrifice, he offered Brahma and all other beings. All those beings merged into the fire. Then, when Bandasuran came and stood before him, he also offered him into the fire. Afterwards, that fire became a Lingam and came to be known as Mayana Lingam. The sacrificial pit where the fire was made to destroy Bandasuran is now the Sivagangai Theertham inside the temple. The 54 Sthalams we have seen so far are those that are praised in separate chapters within Kanchipuranam. There are also Sthalams mentioned as sub-temples within the chapters of Kanchipuranam. We will see them as follows: Panamanisam. The Lord's name is Panamanisar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Vasuki. Yamadharma Lingesam. The Lord's name is Yamadharma Lingeswarar. The worshipper was Yama. Mukkala Gnanesam. The Lord's name is Thrikkala Gnaneswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Munis. Next is Mathangeeswarar Temple. The Lord's name is Mathangeeswarar. The worshipper was Mathanga Muni. Next is Airavathesam. The Lord's name is Airavatheeswarar. The worshipper was Airavatham, the elephant. Next is Mandakanneesam. The Lord's name is Mandakanneeswarar. The Theertham is Aiyrambaiyar Theertham. The worshipper was Mandakanni Muni. Next is Vannisam. The Lord's name is Agneeswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Agni. Next is Saunakesam. The Lord's name is Saunakeswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Saunaka Muni. Next is Kalkeesam, Lakshmeesam. The Lord's name is Kalkeesar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Thirumal and Lakshmi. Next, there is also a Thirugnanasambandar Peruman Temple. This temple has a history related to Thirumetralinathar Temple. Next is Utruketta Muktheeswarar Temple. The Lord's name is Muktheeswarar. Next is Varahakesam. The Lord's name is Varahakeswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Vishnu when he took the Varaha avatar. Next is Kameswaram. The Lord's name is Kameswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Manmathan. Next is Theertheswaram. The Lord's name is Theertheswarar. All Theerthams have worshipped here. Next is Gangavareswaram. The Lord's name is Gangavareswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Varunan and Ganga. Next is Viswanathasam. The Lord's name is Kasi Viswanathar. The Mukthi Mandapam (hall of liberation) is also mentioned in Kanchipuranam. It is believed that those who think of this hall upon waking up in the morning will be freed from bondage and attain liberation. Next is Ramesam. The Lord's name is Rameswarar, Lakshmeeswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Sri Rama and Lakshmi. Next is Sevvantheesam. The Lord's name is Sevvantheeswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Vayu Bhagavan. Next is Parutheesam. The Lord's name is Paritheeswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Surya. Next is Chandreesam. The Lord's name is Chandreeswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Chandra. Next is Muktheesam. The Lord's name is Muktheeswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Vamadevar. Next is Romachareswarar Temple, also called Romacharesam. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Romacha Muni. Next is Kausikesam, worshipped by Kausiki Muni. The Lord's name is Kausikeswarar, Sokkeeswarar. Next is Mahakalesam. The Lord's name is Mahakaleswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by the snake Mahakalan. Next is Devasenapathisam. The Lord's name is Devasenapatheeswarar. This is a Sthalam where Murugapperuman worshipped Lord Shiva. Next is Markandesam. The Lord's name is Markandeswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Markandeya Maharishi. Next is Mangalesam. The Lord's name is Mangaleswarar. The Theertham is Mangala Theertham. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Mangalai, a friend of Kamakshi Devi. Next is Ramanathesam. The Lord's name is Ramanathar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Sri Rama. Next is Vellaikambam. The Lord's name is Vellaikambar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Brahma. This Shiva Lingam is located in a separate shrine in Ekambaranathar Temple. Next is Kallaikambam.

The Lord's name is Mayavaneswarar. The worshipper was Mayavan. Next is Nallakambam. The Lord's name is Nallakambar. The worshipper was Rudra. These two temples are located inside the Ekambareswarar Temple. Next is Valesam. The Lord's name is Valeswarar. The worshipper was Vali. Next is Rishabesam. The Lord's name is Rishabeswarar. The worshipper was Rishabhadevar. Next is Ganganesam. The Lord's name is Pathaleeswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by the Goddess. When the Goddess came to Kanchi to worship the Lord, she consecrated a Shiva Lingam and adorned herself with a golden bangle. Therefore, it is called Ganganesam. Next is Kadakkesam. The Goddess, who adorned herself with a golden bangle in Ganganesam, consecrated and worshipped a Shiva Lingam here and wore a bracelet. Therefore, it is called Kadakkesam. Next is Vinduveesam. The Lord's name is Vinduveesar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Thirumal. Next is Agastheeswaram. The Lord's name is Agastheeswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Agastya Muni. Next is Mathala Mathavesam. The Lord's name is Mathala Matheswarar. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Thirumal. The Sahasralingeswarar Shiva Lingam, which has 1008 Shiva Lingams, is also seen. This temple is mentioned in Kanchipuranam. Next is Lingapesam. The Lord's name is Lingapesar. This temple, which has only a Shiva Lingam, is mentioned as Lingapesam in Kanchipuranam. Next is Mallikarjunar Temple. The Lord's name is Mallikarjuneswarar. Next is Viswaksenam. The Lord's name is Bhairavar Viswakseneswarar. He is in the Soliswarar temple. This is a Sthalam worshipped by Viswaksenar. Only the Bhairavar shrine is here. The 43 Sthalams mentioned above are temples mentioned within the existing chapters of Kanchipuranam, not as separate chapters. Besides these, there are many other temples not included in Kanchipuranam. They are Agastheeswarar Temple, Anantha Rudresam, Aram Valartheeswarar Temple, Chidambareswarar Temple, Ganikandeesam, Mandaleswarar Temple, Nagaresam Keezharaja Veethi, Nagaresam Mettutheru, Rudrakotteeswarar Keneri Kuppam and Pillaiyarpalayam Rudrakotteeswarar, Sukleswarar Temple, Valakkartheeswarar, Veerabhadrar, Virupaksheeswarar Temple, Anatha Rudresam, Dharmayoga Gnana Siddhisam, Ekambaranatham, Kalathinatham, Lingapesam in Kamakottam, Lingapesam in Kachchapesam, Lingapesam in Kayarohanam, Lingapesam in Mahakalesam, Maha Rudresam, Mekkandeesam, Muktheswarar, Mukthisam, Nagalingam, 108 Shiva Lingam, Siddhisam in Panamoodeesam, Dharmesam, Sikkandeesam, Vedapureeswarar, Vilvanatheswarar, Viswanatham, Yugasiddhisam, and many other temples where the Lord is enshrined. These are all Shiva Sthalams located within and around the city of Kanchi. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 13 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam Periyapuranam Part 221 Mummaiyaal Ulaganda Sarukkam Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. We are contemplating the special features of Kanchipuram. In the Kanchipuranam, in the 55th chapter called Thiruvekamba Padalam, the city's greatness, the deity's special features, the place's special features, the sacred water's special features, and the sacred tree's special features are described as told by Lord Shiva. Lord Shiva tells the Devas the following: "Oh Mahārudra, the Veda, which is the Brahma Gnana that clearly explains the truth, has sprouted and flourished here as a mango tree. We, who are the Lord of that Veda, reside at its root. The Veda, which removes ignorance, is in the form of sound, and the mango tree (Sootam) here is in the form of meaning. Know that I am the Lord of that Veda. In this place, Kanchipuram, those with wisdom call my name Ekambanathan, as I am enshrined under the mango tree. This mantra of five letters, Ekambanathan, which is the root mantra, will cut off the five great sins," says Lord Shiva. The Lord says that Ekambanathan is the five-letter sacred mantra. In Tamil, when counting the letters of a word, it is customary to exclude the consonant-vowel combinations. Accordingly, if we remove the consonant-vowel combinations 'm' and 'n' from the word 'Ekambanathan', we get five letters. Therefore, the Lord says it becomes a Panchaksharam. Furthermore, Lord Shiva continues, "This mantra, Ekambanathan, is more auspicious than the five types of Brahma mantras and the Panchaksharam." Lord Shiva says that those who chant the mantra Ekambanathan, which is superior to all mantras, will attain liberation. This sacred mantra, Ekambanathan, is to be taught only to those who have studied and understood all the texts that explain the four types of benefits: dharma, artha, kama, and moksha. The Lord states that even those who utter this mantra, even in times of distress or by mistake, will become dear to him. Hearing this, the Devas request Lord Shiva, "Oh Lord, please explain to us, your humble servants, the glory of this Kanchi Sthalam and the glory of the mango tree where you are enshrined." Lord Shiva explains in detail to the Devas as follows: "Oh Devas, among words, the word 'Ka' is excellent. 'Ka' means Brahma. Since I, who am Brahma, have been embraced (anjikkappattathal), meaning attained, this city will achieve unchanging glory. Because it was embraced by 'Ka,' which is Brahma, it is named Kanchi. Wise people refer to the navel of women as Kanchi. Kanchipuram is the navel of Mother Earth, who is clothed by the ocean. Therefore, it is also called Kanchi. Furthermore, 'Ka' signifies head, bliss, and Brahma Devan, among other things. 'Anjithal' signifies worship, attainment, etc. If 'Ka' is taken as head, it means Kanchi has the special quality of being worthy of worship as the foremost, like a head, by everyone. If 'Ka' is taken as bliss, it means that those who permanently reside in Kanchipuram will attain supreme bliss. If 'Ka' is taken as Brahma Devan, and 'Anji' as worship, then it means this Sthalam was worshipped by Brahma Devan. Therefore, it gets the name Kanchi. Hence, it is also called Brahmalokam. This city, desired by Devas, is filled with those who live in the bliss of liberation. Therefore, it is called Devapuram. The city that cannot be destroyed by enemies in war is called Ayodhya. Ayodhya is a place where kings reside. But it is not a place for Jivanmuktas (liberated souls). However, Kanchipuram is a place where Jivanmuktas reside. Therefore, Kanchi is superior to Ayodhya. Thus, Ayodhya, which signifies being full of great light, Brahmalokam, and Devapuram, are all Kanchipuram and no other city. Now, listen as I tell you the glory of the mango tree," Lord Shiva begins to say. "For the great benefit of this world, the nature of that mango tree, which has roots growing upwards (aroganam) and branches growing downwards (avaroganam), and which emanates the fragrance of wisdom, will lead one to liberation if understood clearly. That mango tree, bearing many fruits, near the banks of the Kampa river, is the Vedic mango tree, full of sound. The 14 Shastras are spread over that mango tree like jasmine vines. Kamakshi, the chief of all Shastras, is fond of those jasmine vines. We are the independent Lord of that mango tree, which does not perish even at the end of a Yuga. The flawless Vedas are my words. There is no difference between the word and its meaning. Therefore, there is no difference between me and this mango tree. I reside as a Lingam under this mango tree. Until yesterday, no one had seen me. Now, you can see me, as I am enshrined here with Parvati Devi. All divine names are my names. Among those names, the thousand names starting with Bhavan are excellent. Among those thousand names, the names Sambhu, Mayobu, Sankaran, Mayakkan, Sivan, Sivatharan are sweet. Among all those names, the name Ekambanathan, which is praised and glorified above all, is most dear to me." Having said this, Lord Shiva then, at the request of the Devas, began to describe the greatness of the Kampa river. "Oh Devas, I have eight forms. One of those forms is water. Water purifies everything in the world. That water flows here in the form of a river for the salvation of all living beings. The clear water of this river is not visible to everyone, just like me. This Kampa river flows deep beneath the earth, just as I reside in the minds of Shiva Yogis. The word 'Pa' means purity, thought, and protection. This river is called Kampa because the waters of Ganga and other rivers, called 'Kam,' immerse themselves in it and attain purity ('Pa') to purify those who immerse themselves in it and remove the dirt from their bodies and their sins. The name 'Kampa' in Sanskrit is transformed into 'Kampai' in Tamil. The Theerthams in Ganga, Mahanadi, Yamuna, Saraswati, and other rivers are not equal to the Theertham in the Kampai river. Even the grass and trees in the areas where the wind blows after touching the Theertham of the Kampa river will attain liberation. Those who have fallen from the state of Yoga and Tapas will be born as fish and other aquatic creatures in this Kampai river and then attain liberation. All the trees on the banks of the Kampa river are Rudras who have come from Rudralokam. Wherever they die, if the Theertham of the Kampa river is sprinkled on their heads at the time of their death, they will attain the bliss of liberation." Thus, Lord Shiva describes the glories of the city of Kanchi, saying that it is surrounded by the glorious Kampai river. The city of Kanchi has 12 sacred names: Bhuvanacharam, Mummoorthi Vasam, Vindupuram, Kanchi, Kalisithu, Layasithu, Sakala Siddhi, Thapomayapuram, Brahmapuram, Adipeedam, Kannikkappu, and Sivapuram. It is called Bhuvanacharam because it is the essence that brings benefits in all three worlds. Lord Shiva tells the Trimurtis who were worshipping him, "You, the Trimurtis, are worshipping me, who manifests as the universe in the forms of earth, water, fire, air, ether, moon, sun, and life. Therefore, it is called Mummoorthi Vasam." Furthermore, he continues, "In the Meghavahana Kalpam, Varadarajan, who has lotus-like eyes, worshipped me. Therefore, it is called Vishnupuram, Vindupuram. We have already explained the reason for the name Kanchipuram. It is called Kalisithu because it protects from the afflictions of Kali Purusha even in the Kali Yuga. It is called Layasithu because this city becomes my dance hall at the end of the Yuga. It is called Sakala Siddhipuram because it grants all siddhis without hindrance. This city, which is the Parabrahma itself, blessed by Parvati Devi who performed penance here, is called Thapomayapuram. Since Brahma Devan performs appropriate Soma Yagas for me here, it is called Brahmapuram. Because it is the foremost seat, it is called Adipeedam. Since Kamakshi Devi, who is still called a virgin even though she has given birth to all beings, protects this city, it is called Kannikkappu. Whoever has my darshan in this place, without any distinction, attains the bliss of Shiva. Therefore, it is called Sivapuram. Lord Shiva spoke with his divine mouth, saying, "If one wakes up in the morning and recites these 12 sacred names that I have mentioned, they will be freed from terrible dreams, painful diseases, poverty, and other miseries, and by my grace, they will attain the blissful abode of liberation. I have explained this to you out of compassion." When does Lord Shiva say all these things? As we saw earlier when looking at the history and glory of the Kanchipuram Sthalam called Kachi Mayanam, Lord Shiva performed a yaga during the destruction of Bandasuran, offering all the Devas into the fire and destroying everyone, and then destroying Bandasuran who remained. After that, Lord Shiva, who created Lalita Devi, his Shiva Shakti, then blessed Lalita Devi to begin creation again. At that time, Lalita Devi created Brahma Devan from her right eye, Vishnu from her left eye, and Rudra, who has a third eye, from her third eye, to perform the three functions of creation, preservation, and destruction. Similarly, she performed other creations. At that time, when these Trimurtis were worshipping Lord Shiva and Lalita Devi, Lord Shiva explains all the glories of Kanchipuram. This is found in the Thriyekamba Padalam of Kanchipuranam. Thus, Kanchipuram has countless glories. We have tried to contemplate a small part of it. Now, we will continue to contemplate the glory of Kanchi city as sung by Sekkizhar Peruman in the Puranam of Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 14 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam Periyapuranam Part 222 Mummaiyaal Ulaganda Sarukkam Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. Sekkizhar Peruman, who sang about the glories of Thondai Nadu, now begins to sing about the special features of Kanchi city. Remembering all the richness of Thondai Nadu that we have already discussed in great detail, and understanding and recalling all the glories of the Shiva temples and Saivite traditions of Thondai Nadu, Sekkizhar Peruman begins to sing that the glorious city of Kanchipuram is located in such a glorious land. Kanchipuram possesses the special quality of providing true wealth. It is a place that bestows the imperishable wealth of liberation, which is called "meyvalam" (true wealth), in the great and glorious land of Thondai Nadu. There are seven cities in our sacred land of Bharath that are known to grant liberation. Kanchipuram is the foremost among these seven cities. Therefore, Sekkizhar Peruman sings about it as a city that bestows true wealth. "Nagaresu Kanchi" (Kanchi among cities) is also a famous saying that we use. Sekkizhar Peruman sings "Yaavarum Ettum" (worshipped by all) to indicate that people of all religions live in such a glorious Kanchipuram. This place has 20 very important Shiva Sthalams and eight Vaishnava Sthalams. Not only Saivites and Vaishnavites, but also Buddhists and Jains have praised Kanchipuram. The epic Manimegalai speaks of the greatness of Kanchipuram in places like "Kachi Magaranam Pukka Kathai" and "Thavaththiram Uraiththa Kathai." Therefore, it is known that Kanchi itself has had various divisions like Vishnu Kanchi, Shiva Kanchi, Jain Kanchi, and Buddhist Kanchi in different periods. This Kanchipuram is a city that remains steadfast even when all worlds are destroyed. It does not perish even in the deluge. Therefore, it is called Pralayasithu. Once, Brahma Devar placed Kanchi city on one side of a balance and all other sacred places on the other side, and the side where Kanchipuram was placed was found to be much heavier, indicating its greater glory, as mentioned in Kanchipuranam. Now, Sekkizhar Peruman begins to sing about the divine history of the Goddess performing Shiva Puja in Kanchipuram according to the Agamas. The next verse is the 49th. "Anatholnagar Ambigai tham Perumanai Maana archanaiyaal oru kaalathu vazhipattu Oonamil aram anekam ulagu uyya vaiththa Menmai poondhavap perumaiyai arindhava vilambil" (If I were to describe the greatness of that ancient city where the Goddess worshipped her Lord with glorious worship at one time, and established many flawless acts of righteousness for the salvation of the world), says the 49th verse, and then he sings the 50th verse. "Velli maalvarai Kailaiyil veetrirundharuli Thulluvar punal veniyar arul seyyath thozhuthu Thellu vaaimaiyin agamat thiranellam theriya Ullavaaru kettarulinaal ulagai aludaiyaal" (The Goddess, who rules the world, worshipped the Lord with flowing Ganga in his matted locks, who was seated in the great silver mountain Kailash, and asked him to reveal all the clear truths of the Agamas). These are the meanings of these two verses. The meaning of these two verses is as follows: If I were to begin to tell the greatness of that ancient city of Kanchi where Umaiyammaiyaar, at one time, worshipped her Lord Shiva with glorious worship and established many acts of righteousness for the salvation of the world, then the Lord Shiva, who is seated in the great silver mountain Kailash and has the Ganga in his matted locks, graciously revealed the clear truths of the Agamas to Umaiyammaiyaar, who rules the world, when she worshipped him and asked him to reveal them. This is the meaning of these two sacred verses. Now, the detailed meaning of these sacred verses. Kanchi city is an ancient city. Here, Umaiyammaiyaar, at one time, performed worship according to the methods prescribed by the Shiva Agamas, without any deficiency. Sekkizhar Peruman mentions "oru kaalathu" (at one time) here, which Alalasundaran Pillai interprets as referring to the worship performed by Radha Devi earlier and then by Parvati Devi. Thirugnanasambandar Peruman, in the seventh verse of the 114th hymn "Thiruvekambam" in the third Thirumurai, mentions "Muthira Mangai thavam seytha kaalame munbu Mangai thavam seytha kaalame" (The time when the mature maiden performed penance, the time when the maiden performed penance earlier). This means that just as the Goddess performed penance in the Himalayas to attain Lord Shiva as her husband, here on the banks of the Kampa river, the Goddess performed penance and Shiva Puja towards Lord Shiva. This is the meaning. Thus, Sekkizhar Peruman mentions that she performed "Maana archanai" (glorious worship) at one time. "Maana archanai" means glorious worship. This is a puja performed without any deficiency according to the methods prescribed in the Shiva Agamas. Therefore, he says so. Furthermore, the Goddess resides here, performing many acts of righteousness. Those who perform righteousness will attain its benefits. The righteousness performed by the Goddess here is for the salvation of the world. The Goddess performs acts of righteousness and Shiva Puja for the salvation of the world. Sekkizhar Peruman says, "I am beginning to tell the divine history of the Goddess's penance," and he begins to sing that divine history for all of us to hear and be saved. Kailash mountain is called "Velli maal varai Kailai" (great silver mountain Kailash). Kailash mountain is the most excellent among the high peaks of the great silver mountain. It is also known as "Nodiththan Malai." "Nodiththal" means to destroy. "Nodiththan" means one who destroys. "Oozhithorum oozhi mutrum uyar pon nodiththan malai" (The high golden mountain that destroys Yuga after Yuga) is a Thevaram by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. We have seen the glories of Thirukailash mountain in the "Thirumalai Sarkam" in the section "Thirumalai Sirappu." Lord Shiva is seated on such a glorious Thirukailash mountain. He is seated with Parvati Devi. At that time, Umaiyammaiyaar, who rules the world, worships Lord Shiva and asks him. Sekkizhar Peruman refers to her as "Ulagai aludaiyaal" (one who rules the world), which indicates that Uma Devi asked for the benefit of all living beings in the world. Umaiyammaiyaar asks Lord Shiva, worshipping him, to graciously reveal all the clear truths of the Agamas for the salvation of all living beings. Here, Sekkizhar Peruman has sung "Thellu vaaimaiyin agamat thiranellam" (all the clear truths of the Agamas). Sivakavimani Ayya writes the following commentary on this: The Veda says, "Isana Sarva Vidyanam" (Isana is the Lord of all knowledge). Accordingly, Isana is the chief of all arts. That Lord has revealed countless Shastras according to the different levels of maturity of living beings. Among all those Shastras, the Vedas and Agamas are the most supreme. Gnana Shastras say that there are two types of texts: Vedic texts and Saivite texts, and that the Veda and Agama are the true texts of the Lord. Among these two texts, the Veda is a general text. It speaks of various things like "Annam Brahma," "Vishnu Brahma," etc., according to the capacity and maturity of living beings. The Agama, however, without being general, clearly states the ultimate truth that Shiva is the supreme God for those who have attained Shakti Nipada (descent of divine grace). Therefore, what the Veda generally calls Brahma, the Agama specifically calls Shiva.
