Her devotion to the Lord intensified and soared. Her divine body trembled. Small drops of sweat appeared on her forehead. Pearl necklaces swayed on her breasts. Her slender waist wilted. Buzzing bees, immersed in honey, hummed in her hair. The Mekala ornament around her waist jingled. Golden anklets and bells jingled. Golden bracelets on her hands clinked. Like a flowering creeper that sprouted and sent out two tender shoots on either side, embracing a golden mountain peak, the Goddess, like a statue of emerald, quickly rose. She knelt on her right knee on the fragrant altar, and with both her hands, she tightly embraced her Lord, her husband. As soon as the Goddess embraced Him tightly with her mountain-like breasts and her red hands adorned with bangles, with overflowing, endless love, Ekambaranathar, the Lord, revealed His wondrous, blissful, luminous divine body, which dispels the darkness of ignorance, bearing the marks of the bangles on the Empress's red hands and the marks of her breasts. How wondrous, wondrous, wondrous it is! Our Lord, whose divine body is the supreme, blissful light, who is beyond form, formless, atomic, mountainous, existent, non-existent, darkness, light, and everything else, pervading everywhere, bore the marks of the bangles on the red hands and the marks of the breasts of the Goddess, whose hair was adorned with fragrant flowers. How astonishing! Our Lord, whose divine body is the supreme, blissful light, who resides in the mind as the size of a thumb, and who effortlessly moves all the universes, bore the marks of the bangles on the red hands and the marks of the breasts of the Goddess, who possesses great compassion and a gentle smile. How astonishing! Our Lord, whose divine body is the supreme, blissful light, whom Shiva yogis, who can control the Rechaka and Puraka breaths and sleep without sleeping, behold with their eye of true knowledge, bore the marks of the bangles on the red hands and the marks of the breasts of the Goddess, who speaks sweetly like a female cuckoo. How wonderful! My Father, whose divine body is the supreme, blissful light, who is always of one nature, free from thought and forgetfulness, immersed in the Self, and easily accessible to the love of Jnanis, bore the marks of the bangles on the red hands and the marks of the breasts of the Goddess, who has tender, sprout-like small feet. How astonishing! By merely thinking of His divine feet in the mind, the Lord, whose divine body is the supreme, blissful light, casts away the great karmic births of those who think of Him. How wonderful that He bore the marks of the bangles on the red hands and the marks of the breasts of the Goddess, the mother of all worlds. It is a great wonder that the red coral mountain, which bent the golden mountain as a bow, the eternal, beginningless, and endless Vedic form, became soft and slender due to the embrace of a flowering creeper's red hands and breasts. As soon as Thiruekambamudaiyar (Ekambaranathar) bore the marks of the bangles on His red hands and the marks of the breasts on His divine body, overflowing with compassion and attaining supreme bliss, all living beings in the heavens and on earth rejoiced greatly. The earth and the heavens shone without blemish. Countless musical instruments resounded. The gentle breeze wafted with a new fragrance. The dust settled throughout the ocean-bound world. The homa fire rose in a swirling column, radiating light. A shower of golden flowers fell incessantly. The Vedas, with their many meters, all rose individually, rejoiced, and resounded. All the worlds, without suffering from delusion, rejoiced. As all this was happening, the Devas, led by Indra, and the Asuras, all came there with their consorts, overjoyed. Brahma, seated on a lotus, came there with Saraswati. Vishnu, adorned with a cool, fragrant Tulasi garland, came there with Lakshmi. They came rejoicing, saying, "We have received supreme bliss today." The glorious Narada Muni and other Devarishis, and the Brahmarishis led by Vasishta, came in countless joyful crowds, with their tridents and other staffs, and their matted locks shining. Many Shaktis, the daughters of Diti, Aditi, and Daksha, Yoginis, Arundhati, Lopamudra, Anasuya, and other women, all came. The four great sages, Sanaka, Sanatkumara, Sanandana, and Sanatana, came. Agastya Muni, Durvasa Muni, Upamanyu Muni, the heroic Bhutaganas, Rudras, Ganadhipathis, Thirunandi Devar, and everyone else gathered there. Thus, everyone gathered there and continuously beheld the wondrous, luminous, blissful divine body of Ekambaranathar, who had softened and yielded to the Empress's embrace, and they roared "Hara Hara, Jaya Jaya." They plunged into the ocean of supreme bliss, rolled and tossed, and with their eyes, which had attained a great, rare fortune, their flower-like eyes shed tears of joy. Their bodies were covered with goosebumps due to the overflowing love, and they mingled like water absorbed by iron. Reflecting on the great compassion of the Lord's divine grace, their five senses were immersed in bliss. With their hands joined above their heads, Vishnu, who holds the chakra shining like a thousand suns, and Brahma, regretted that they did not have a thousand eyes to behold this wondrous sight. Indra, whose entire body had a thousand eyes, also wept, saying that even these thousand eyes were not enough. In this manner, everyone bowed down, rose, danced in joy, and praised and blessed with a roar like the seven oceans. At that time, only Lord Shiva, the deity adorned with the Konrai flower, knew the supreme bliss they experienced. Who else could know it? At that moment, Lord Paramashiva, who is difficult for the Trimurtis – Brahma, Vishnu, and Rudra – to behold, appeared from the beautiful, luminous Lingam, shining with the wondrous divine body bearing the marks of the Goddess's bangles on her red hands and the marks of her saffron-smeared breasts. The Lord appeared from the beautiful, luminous Lingam. With a gentle smile that radiated moonlight, the Lord established the great flood that came in the Kamba River there by the name of Sarva Teertham. The Lord said to the Empress, who had embraced Him, "See, I am supremely pleased because you have embraced Me." The Empress, the sole sovereign, her two eyes filled with joy at the wondrous divine body of her Lord, released her tightly embracing hands, rose from the earth, and with overflowing love, made an anjali. She plunged into the boundless ocean of supreme bliss, which surged as she beheld the pure, radiant, gem-like divine body, and experienced the bliss of Shiva. Then, the Empress praised Lord Shiva. "Salutations to the pure flame of knowledge, whom the tall one (Vishnu) and Brahma could not see. Salutations to the ocean of great grace, who bestowed fearlessness upon me, Your servant. Salutations to the Supreme Being, who is distinct from the 36 Tattvas, from the earth upwards. Salutations, salutations to the divine being who resides under a mango tree, emanating the fragrance of knowledge. Salutations, I, Your servant, have received a fortune that no one has ever received. Salutations to the one who creates, protects, and sustains all the universes in arrays. Salutations to the Lord who performs the Panchakritya Tandavam in the golden hall, with the heroic anklets resounding. Salutations, salutations to the eternal one who resides under a mango tree, emanating the fragrance of abundant flowers. Salutations, salutations to the one who has the eight forms: earth, water, fire, air, ether, moon, sun, and the master. Salutations, salutations to the one who has three eyes: Soma (moon), Surya (sun), and Agni (fire). Salutations to the Gnana Murthy who resides under a mango tree, blossoming with the fragrance of knowledge. Salutations to Lord Neelakanta. Salutations to the apple of my eye. Salutations to the one who bestows great grace even upon those who worship Him forgetfully. Salutations, salutations to the benevolent one who resides under the Sutha tree, which is the form of the Vedas. Salutations to the ancient one who existed before all things that were born first. Salutations to the new one who will be born after all things that will be born later. Salutations to the boundless, complete one whom the ignorant cannot comprehend. Salutations to the pure, conscious Thiruekamban. Shiva Shiva, praise, praise!" Thus, the Goddess, with her fragrant, flower-adorned hair, praised Him with a melting heart and boundless love. The Lord showered His flood of grace there. "Praise to my Father who resides in Ekambam! Praise to the one whose body is half woman, half man!" We will continue to contemplate this. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 23 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periya Puranam, Part 231. Mummaiyaal Ulaganda Sarukkam, Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. In Kanchipuram, on a day when the Empress was performing Shiva Puja according to the Agamas, the Lord, intending to perform a divine play, caused a great flood to surge in the Kamba River. Unable to stop the surging flood, the Empress embraced Ekambaranathar tightly with her bangle-adorned hands and her divine breasts. Ekambaranathar softened His divine body to be gentle to the Mother's divine breasts and bangle-adorned divine hands. Sekkizhar Peruman explains this in the 64th verse as follows: "When the Mountain Princess, out of loving fear, tightly embraced the Lord of the Devas residing in the mango tree, pressing Him with her mountain-like breasts and bangle-adorned hands, with surging love, the Lord, who wears the hide of a murderous elephant, softened His divine body to be gentle to her beautiful forehead, her divine breasts, and her tender sprout-like hands, as if He were a person of resolute principles." Now, in the 65th verse, he composes as follows: "Seeing the Lord of Ekambam soften His body as His beloved embraced Him, all stationary and moving beings, from the Devas to all seven types of living beings, their souls and bodies melted and became one, praising Him as 'Ekambar became gentle for our Empress.' The sky showered fragrant flowers, and the Kamba River bowed down and its flood subsided." This is the next verse, the 65th verse. Seeing Ekambaranathar soften His body as His beloved Mother embraced Him, all stationary and moving beings, all seven types of living beings including the Devas, their souls and bodies melted and became one. They praised Him, saying, "Ekambar became gentle for His Empress." The celestial beings showered fragrant Kalpaka flowers. At that very moment, the Kamba River bowed down and its flood subsided. We can recall here what we have already seen: that the Lord stopped the surging flood of the Kamba River by naming it Sarva Teertham and made the river flow underground. When the Empress embraced her beloved Lord, why did joy arise in all living beings? Because the Lord, the thief who hid within every living being as the life of life, was embraced. Therefore, all living beings experienced His softening in their consciousness. This is its meaning. If "soul and body melted and became one," it means the body melted, and the soul within it also melted. Becoming subject to the single emotion of melting, "they themselves became one, and without the cessation of the command, they performed actions of coming and going in the two deeds," as per the second sutra of Sivagnana Bodham. The Lord is present, mingled as all living beings themselves. Therefore, all living beings melted and became one due to the Lord's softening. When Lord Shiva was struck by the Pandyan king's cane on the banks of the Vaigai, and when He was struck while carrying earth for the Puttu, and when the Lord was struck by Arjuna's arrow in the Kailasa mountain range, the stories say that the blows the Lord received were felt by all living beings. Similarly, here, due to the Lord's softening, all living beings have now melted. Now, the 66th verse: "Having bathed in the sacred ash, adorned with matted locks intertwined with the surging Ganga, wearing white ear-ornaments and Rudraksha beads hanging low, with a mind absorbed in the yoga that mingled with them, the Primal Lord, as if performing great penance, bore the marks of the bangles and the impression of the breasts, as the Mother who gave birth to and nourished all worlds bestowed the true fruit." Its meaning:

The Saivite wears pure sacred ash all over his divine body. He has bound his matted locks, intertwined with the surging Ganga River, as his crown. He wears white conch ear-ornaments and Rudraksha beads hanging low in his divine ears. The sacred ash smeared on the Lord's divine body is called Vibhuti. Bhuti means wealth, and Vibhuti means excellent wealth. Sacred ash is the best wealth of all. The Vedas praise "Bhutihi Bhutir Aishwaryam" (sacred ash is wealth, prosperity). The Vedas praise the Lord wearing sacred ash as "Bhasmoddhulita Sarvangam" (one whose entire body is smeared with sacred ash). Now, the Lord wears matted locks. He wears ear-ornaments and beads in his divine ears, so that they hang low and sway. He wears conch ear-ornaments. All these are ascetic forms. These are all the ascetic forms of Lord Shiva. Because in Kanchipuram, the Mother performed Shiva Puja and penance. As a result of that Shiva Puja, the Mother attained the Lord and embraced Him. It is as if the Father also performed penance to be embraced by the Mother and received the fruit of that penance, thus both together bestowed divine grace upon living beings. "What penance did the maiden perform to embrace the Lord?" they would say. "What penance did the generous one perform to marry the maiden?" they would say. This is the 120th verse of the Thirumanappadalam in the Thiruvilaiyadal Puranam. When Meenakshi Ammai and Sundaresa Peruman were married in Madurai, all the people who attended were amazed at the two of them. Some said, "What penance did the generous Sundaresa Peruman perform to marry our Thadathagai Pirattiyar, Meenakshi?" Others said, "What penance did our Meenakshi Devi perform to marry Sundaresa Peruman?" This is how the verse is structured in the Thirumanappadalam of the Thiruvilaiyadal Puranam. Recalling that here, the Mother performed penance and as a result of that penance, she attained the Lord and embraced Him. This verse implies that it is as if the Father also performed penance to be embraced by the Mother and received that fruit, thus both together bestowed divine grace upon living beings. Because the true benefit of the Mother and Father being together is indeed the divine grace they bestow upon living beings, isn't it? Thus, the Mother, who gave birth to all worlds, gave the benefit of touching and embracing her divine body. And the Ekambaranathar bore the marks of her bangles and the impressions of her breasts, which are the distinguishing marks of the Mother's divine body, as His own. Even today, in Kanchipuram, Ekambaranathar appears with the marks of the Mother's bangles and the impressions of her breasts, displaying them as signs of grace. Thus, this verse composed by Sekkizhar Peruman is structured. Now, the next verse. Ekambaranathar, who softened for the Mother's divine breasts, reveals His beautiful bridal form to the Mother. The Lord grants grace to the Mother, saying, "Receive the boons you desire." Then the Mother asks Lord Shiva. To indicate that this Shiva Puja, today's Shiva Puja, is not yet complete, the Mother bows down at the fragrant lotus-like divine feet of Thiruekambar, who is the form of the Vedas. Lord Shiva, who was hidden within the Shiva Linga, now appears and shows the Mother His form as He resides in Kailasa, saying, "Receive the boons you desire." As soon as He said this, the Mother bowed to Lord Shiva, and then she realized that the time to ask for boons, as granted by the Lord, would come only after the Shiva Puja was completed. But today's Shiva Puja has not yet taken place. It is not yet complete. The Mother reveals this to the Lord.

She reveals this by bowing down at the Lord's divine feet with folded hands. The Lord granted, "Receive the boon." But without receiving the boon, the Shiva Puja is now halfway. Therefore, the Mother requests the Lord that the Shiva Puja should be completed. When the Lord said, "Receive the boon," the Shiva Puja is now halfway. Therefore, Ambika requests the Lord that the Shiva Puja should be completed. At that time, Lord Shiva looks at Ambika's lotus-like divine face and says, "Your worship to Us never ends." Lord Shiva blesses, "The Shiva Puja you perform never ends." First, He accepted the portion of the puja performed so far and then appeared openly, revealing His divine form. Now, when Ambika asks for the remaining puja to be completed, He blesses, "Your puja never ends." Sekkizhar Peruman uses the phrase, "Nin poosanai endrum mudivathu illai nambal" (Your worship never ends for Us). The Shiva Puja performed by Ambika never ends. Because Lord Shiva and the Empress are eternal. Ambika performs Shiva Puja for the sake of the lives of the beings she gave birth to, for the benefit of their liberation. And those beings are also eternal. Ambika performs this Shiva Puja for the sake of those eternal beings. Therefore, Ambika and the Lord, being together eternally, should always bestow their divine grace upon living beings eternally. Therefore, Ambika's puja continues eternally without end. This is the meaning of this verse. Not only that, but in the upcoming verse, we should remember that Ambika's first boon is that this puja should remain eternal, and the Lord should joyfully accept that puja. Thus, as Lord Shiva joyfully blessed, now the Empress, our Mother, asks Lord Shiva for some boons for us. We will continue to contemplate them. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 24 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periya Puranam, Part 232. Mummaiyaal Ulaganda Sarukkam, Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. In Kanchipuram, after performing Shiva Puja according to the Shiva Agamas, when Lord Shiva appeared, Kamakshi Devi requested three boons from Lord Shiva. The first boon was that the Shiva Puja she performed should be eternal, and the Lord should accept and bless that Shiva Puja. As the second boon, Kamakshi Devi requested the Lord that she should be able to perform all dharmas in Kanchi city and that He should bless her for it. As the third boon, she requested that even if the residents of Kanchipuram commit any actions other than Shiva Aparadham (offence against Shiva), all those actions should be considered as great penance, and the fruit of that great penance should be bestowed upon them. Thus, Sekkizhar Peruman sings that Ambika stood requesting these three boons from Lord Shiva. We have already seen that Lord Shiva blessed that the Shiva Puja performed by Ambika is an eternal puja. Accordingly, as the first boon, the Mother requests that she should eternally perform Shiva Puja in Kanchipuram, and that the Lord should accept and bless that Shiva Puja. Now, as the second boon, Kamakshi Devi asks the Lord that she should be able to perform all dharmas in Kanchipuram. It is the Mother's duty to protect all living beings through the household life she leads with Lord Shiva. Furthermore, it is the Mother's duty to guide her people in this world in household life. Therefore, the Mother requests this boon. Now, as the third boon, Ambika asks that whatever actions the residents of Kanchipuram perform, whatever karmas they perform, for all those actions, the fruit of great penance should be bestowed upon them. It is a general rule of the Lord's karmic division that each person receives the appropriate fruit for the actions they perform. The Lord bestows the fruits of actions upon each living being according to the actions they have performed. However, in this third boon, Ambika asks Lord Shiva that the residents of Kanchipuram, without considering the rules and exceptions of what they should do and what they should not do, whatever actions they perform, all those actions should be considered as great penance, and the fruit of great penance should be bestowed upon them. Even though it is the Lord's general rule to bestow fruits according to actions, Ambika requests that by the special grace of the Lord, whatever actions the residents of Kanchipuram perform, the fruit of great penance should be bestowed upon them. Because Ambika is the one who removes the births of living beings. She is the mother of all. Therefore, she requested these boons for the sake of her children. The only exception here is that Ambika asks that any actions other than Shiva Aparadham should be considered as penance and the fruit of that penance should be given. What is Shiva Aparadham? Shiva Aparadham includes slander of Shiva, slander of Shiva's devotees, slander of Vedas and Shiva Agamas, and so on. There is no remedy for Shiva Aparadham. Shiva Aparadhams are offenses for which there is no atonement. It is the conclusion of authentic texts that those who commit Shiva Aparadhams will suffer severe punishments and terrible hellish torments, just as Daksha lost his head. Shiva Aparadhams are unforgivable offenses. Therefore, Ambika asks that excluding these offenses, whatever other actions are performed, the fruit of penance should be bestowed upon them. Thus, when Kamakshi Devi requested these three boons, Lord Shiva, who comes on the Rishabha Vahana, granted all three boons to Ambika. Therefore, Kamakshi Devi eternally performs Shiva Puja in Kanchipuram according to the Shiva Agamas, and Lord Shiva accepts and blesses it. This is how the first boon was granted. Now, as the second boon, she requested that she should be able to perform all dharmas in Kanchipuram. Accordingly, the Lord grants this second boon to Ambika. To enable Kamakshi Devi to perform dharmas in Kanchipuram, the Lord gives her two measures of paddy. These two measures of paddy are called "Iga Parathu Iru Nazhi" (two measures for this world and the next). He gives one measure for the dharmas related to this world and one measure for the dharmas related to the next world. With these two measures of paddy given by the Lord, Kamakshi Devi performs 32 dharmas there. The paddy required for the worldly life of living beings is one measure. The second measure of paddy is for performing dharmas like creation, which are necessary for the bliss of the next world. The Lord gives paddy to promote dharmas. Because paddy is not just a seed for food. It is also the seed for the dharmas performed by living beings. "I will give you two measures of paddy as a seed to cultivate truth and dharma every year. With that paddy, may the green crops of life in this world and the next flourish," says the 426th verse of Thazhuva Kuzhaintha Padalam in Kanchi Puranam. Its meaning is that Lord Shiva says to Ambika, "I will give you two measures of paddy as a seed every year for you to cultivate dharmas. With that paddy, may the green crops of life in this world and the next flourish." This is how it is structured in the Kanchi Puranam, granting that boon. Therefore, even today in Kanchi city, Ambika, with the two measures of paddy given by the Lord, cultivates 32 dharmas. Tamil texts like Sendan Divakaram describe what these 32 dharmas are. Sendan Divakaram, a text, has structured these 32 dharmas in verse form. "Athular Salai, Odhuvaarkku Unavu, Arusamayathorkku Unavu, Pasuvukku Vayoorai, Siraichoru, Ayyam, Thinpandam, Makachoru, Makapperu Viththal, Maka Valarththal, Makappal, Aravai Pinam Suduthal, Aravai Thuriyam, Vannar, Navithar, Vaduvai, Poonool, Noi, Marundhu, Kannadi, Kadhoolai, Kan Marundhu, Thalaikku Ennai, Pen Bhogam, Sunnam, Pirar Thuyar Kaaththal, Thanneer Pandhal, Madam, Thadagam, Kaa, Aa Urinchi Naduthari, Eru Viduthal, Vilai Koduthu Kolai Uyir Meettal, Ichcheyal 32 Aram Enba." This is that verse. The first among these is Athular Salai. Establishing a hospital for the very poor is the first dharma. Next, secondly, Odhuvaarkku Unavu. Providing food to students, that is, to Brahmacharis in Gurukulams. Thirdly, Arusamayathorkku Undi. Providing food to people of all six religions, divided into six types. Next, Pasuvukku Vayoorai. Providing food to cows. This is the fourth dharma. Fifth, Siraichoru. Providing food to prisoners. Sixth dharma, Ayyam. Giving alms. "Ayyam ittu un" (Eat after giving alms) is Avvaiyar's saying. Seventh dharma, Thinpandam Koduthal. Giving food items. Eighth dharma, Aravai Choru. That is, providing food to the helpless and unsupported. Ninth dharma, Makapperu Viththal. That is, providing all the necessary items for childbirth. Tenth dharma,

Makam Valarththal, which means raising other people's children. The 11th dharma is Makappal Vaarththal, meaning giving milk to small children to drink. The 12th dharma is Aravai Pinam Suduthal, meaning performing the final rites, such as cremation, for the bodies of those who died without support, according to their traditions. The 13th dharma is Aravai Thuriyam, providing clothes and other necessities to the helpless. The 14th dharma is Sunnam, giving fragrant powders, lime, and so on. The 15th dharma is Noi Marundhu, buying and giving medicines for diseases. The 16th dharma is Vannar, giving donations to laundry workers. The 17th is Navithar, giving donations to barbers and those involved in that profession. The 18th is Kannadi, giving mirrors. The 19th is Kadhoolai, buying and giving ear ornaments to wear in the ears. The 20th dharma is Kan Marundhu, giving medicine to those with impaired eyesight. The 21st dharma is Thalaikku Ennai Koduthal, giving oil for the head. The 22nd dharma is Pen Bhogam, meaning providing necessary assistance to those involved in household life for their household life to flourish. The 23rd dharma is Pirar Thuyar Kaaththal, alleviating the suffering of others in any way possible, speaking comforting words to those who are suffering, mentally wishing for their suffering to cease, and physically helping them to alleviate their suffering. The 24th dharma is Thanneer Pandhal Vaiththal, establishing water stalls. The 25th dharma is Madam, establishing monasteries for the poor and travelers to stay. The 26th dharma is Thadam Amaiththal, meaning establishing water bodies like ponds. The 27th dharma is creating trees and groves, creating forests. The 28th dharma is Aa Urinchi Naduthari, meaning planting stones along roads and other places where animals travel, so that the animals can rub their bodies against these stones and find comfort. The 29th dharma is feeding animals and providing them with necessary provisions at appropriate times. The 31st dharma is Vilai Koduthu Uyir Kaaththal, saving a life that is about to be killed, at any cost. The 32nd dharma is Kannikathanam. Thus, various Tamil texts like Sendan Divakaram and Abhidhana Chintamani describe these 32 dharmas. However, in the Kanchi Puranam, the author of Kanchi Puranam describes the following as the 32 dharmas performed by Ambika. First among them is establishing places for performing the five yagnas (five types of sacrifices) performed by householders: Deva Yagam, Pitru Yagam, Bhuta Yagam, Manuda Yagam, and Brahma Yagam. Giving charity to the poor, establishing monasteries for renunciates, providing homes for householders, giving them necessary items for household life, establishing water stalls, creating groves, giving to beggars what they ask for, protecting the blind and other physically challenged from their sufferings, giving medicines to cure terrible diseases, raising small children, giving lime, giving betel leaf and areca nut (thamboolam), giving oil for the head, giving bathing powders like araippu and shikai, giving sleeping mats and other bedding materials, giving land (bhoothanam), giving cows (gothanam), giving away daughters in marriage (kannikathanam), arranging marriages, lighting lamps, settling someone's endless debts and freeing them from it, giving necessary assistance to Shiva's devotees,

Giving Rudraksha, giving sacred ash, giving items for Lord Shiva, giving Shiva Puja vessels, teaching Vedas and Shiva Agamas, teaching Shiva Puranas (i.e., making them read), giving Shiva Upadesam to develop true devotion to Lord Shiva's feet, and giving refuge to those who seek it. Kamakshi Devi performs these 32 dharmas in Kanchi with the two measures of paddy given by the Lord, as the Kanchi Puranam elaborates. This is the boon related to the second request made by Ambika, that she should be able to perform all dharmas in Kanchi city and that He should bless her for it. Now, as the third boon, we saw that she asks that any actions performed by living beings in Kanchi city should be accepted as penance, and that any actions other than Shiva Aparadham should be accepted as penance and grace should be bestowed. Lord Shiva also grants this boon. Sekkizhar Peruman, while singing the glories of Thondai Nadu in this Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam, sings that Thondai Nadu is a land where pure people, whose minds never even dream of evil, reside. Is that not due to the boon received by Kamakshi Devi? If this applies to the entire Thondai Nadu, then it also applies to Kanchipuram, doesn't it? Therefore, the Lord also grants the third boon requested by the Mother. But if one asks if the residents of Kanchipuram commit evil deeds, if they do commit evil deeds, then there are possibilities for such incidents to occur in Kanchipuram, such as committing evil deeds forgetfully, committing them due to others' influence, committing them losing self-control, or committing them by those of other religions who are not connected to Shiva, or by people from other countries who come here. Therefore, out of compassion for such people, the Mother asks that even those evil deeds should be transformed into penance and yield the fruit of penance, and the Father also grants it. We will continue to contemplate this. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 25 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam Periyapuranam Part 233 Mummaiyal Ulaganda Sarukkam Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. In Kanchipuram, Kamakshi Devi performs Shiva Puja according to Shiva Agamas and receives three boons from the Lord. At this place, in the Kanchi Puranam, Lord Shiva, Ekambaranathar, promises Ambika that He will marry Kamakshi Devi on the Panguni Uttiram festival day. Accordingly, the divine marriage of Ekambaranathar is still celebrated every year on Panguni Uttiram at the Ekambaranathar Temple. We previously saw the news that Kamakshi Ammaiyar came to Kanchipuram on the Poora star in the month of Aippasi. The divine marriage of the Lord and the Goddess takes place on Panguni Uttiram. Now, Sekkizhar Peruman continues to sing. Thus, the Goddess received rare and great boons. According to the boons she received, so that her Lord would be pleased and bestow grace, Kamakshi Devi, residing in Tirukkama Kottam in Kanchipuram, performs Shiva Puja, and to ensure that the benefits of this Shiva Puja reach all living beings, she fosters 32 acts of charity. Why? Because she wished to show how the dharma of household life should be lived, and out of compassion to make that household dharma flourish in the world, and for all living beings to attain salvation, Kamakshi Devi resided in Tirukkama Kottam. The first boon Kamakshi Devi asked for was that she should always perform Shiva Puja according to Shiva Agamas, and that the Lord should accept it. Accordingly, accepting that first boon, and with the Lord's grace, Kamakshi Devi resides in Tirukkama Kottam in Kanchipuram and performs Shiva Puja eternally. The Tirukkama Kottam in Kanchipuram mentioned here is currently the Kamakshi Ambika Temple in Kanchipuram, where Kamakshi Devi is centrally enshrined. Sekkizhar Peruman refers to it as Kacchi Tirukkama Kottam in the Periyapuranam. Other Saivite texts also refer to the Kamakshi Ammaiyar Temple, which is centrally located in Kanchipuram, as Kacchi Tirukkama Kottam. Residing in that temple, Kacchi Tirukkama Kottam, Kamakshi Devi performs Shiva Puja eternally to this day. When Kamakshi Devi came to Kanchipuram according to the boon granted by the Lord, did not Padmanagam request the Goddess to reside in its bilam (cave)? The place where that bilam is located is currently the Kamakshi Devi Temple in Kanchipuram. That Tirukkama Kottam still has a bilam. Near that bilam, Kamakshi Devi performs Shiva Puja eternally. This Kamakshi Devi Temple stands as a unique temple for Ambika in the center of Kanchipuram. Our Samayacharyas never worshipped in any temple other than Lord Shiva's temple. They had the tradition of worshipping only in Lord Shiva's temples. The Kamakshi Ambika Temple in Kanchipuram is worthy of the glory of being worshipped by our three great Saivite Samayacharyas. Sundaramurthy Swamigal's Tevaram, "Taiyalaal ulaguyya vaitta kaarirum pozhil Kacchi moodoor Kaamakkottam undaaga neer poy ooridum picchai kolvadenne oonakkaantan thaliyuleere," sings about the Kamakshi Devi Temple. This is also known as Kamakkotti Peedam and Adi Peedam. Only in this temple is there a separate shrine for Ambika. Apart from this temple, no other Shiva temple in Kanchipuram, including the Tiruekambaranathar Temple on the outskirts of Kanchipuram, has a separate shrine for Ambika. In the Shiva temples of Kanchipuram, there is no separate shrine for Ambika. In the Kamakshi Devi Temple, which is the central deity of Kanchi, Ambika has a separate temple and performs Shiva Puja, bestowing grace upon devotees. Furthermore, the glories of Kamakshi Devi's temple are explained by Sri Madhava Sivagnana Swamigal in the Veerattakasa Padalam of the Kanchi Puranam. The place where Kamakshi Devi eternally performs penance on the banks of the tirtha known as Ulagaani Tirtha possesses countless glories. The place where the bilam is located in the Kamakshi Devi Temple is considered the navel of the earth goddess, who wears the oceans as her garment. The bilam where Kamakshi Devi resides is a good place, a subtle bilakasha, that is, a subtle ethereal space. It is also a great gracious chidakasha (consciousness space). Furthermore, it is the root cause and divine form that destroys the karmas of the devotees of Uma Ammaiyar. It is complete and perfect. It is both existence (sat) and consciousness (chit). The human mind, like a potter's wheel, revolves incessantly. This is the place where such a revolving and struggling mind finds rest. It is the essence of Vedanta. It is the supreme light, Shiva, pure, blissful, and immaculate, whom the Shiva Yogis, filled with deep penance, visualize inwardly in their minds as this vast space, like a full pot. In the same way that Shiva Yogis visualize inwardly in their minds, they will outwardly and openly visualize the same vast space, the same parakasha, at the bilam in the Kamakshi Devi Temple in Kanchipuram. Once, those who perform charity at the place where the bilam is located, for the fulfillment of their desires, the benefit of their charity will be a crore times multiplied. If the benefit of charity performed with desire is multiplied a crore times, can the benefit of charity performed without desire be described? Its benefit cannot be described at all. That is the bilam where the Mother, who gave birth to the elder Ganesha, the elephant-faced one, who pours forth ichor, and Lord Muruga, resides. That place is known by the descriptive name Kamakoti. It is called Kamakoti because all desired objects multiply a crore times, all desired objects are obtained a crore times. Kubera is the lord of all great wealth. Kubera is called Kamattalaivar (Lord of Desires) because he grants desired wealth. It is called Kamakoti because it created a crore such Kamattalaivars (Lords of Desires) like Kubera. It is the great luminous bilam where Kamakshi Devi, the Goddess who is the supreme light, Lord Shiva, who pervades everywhere and transcends everything, resides, having embraced Him with her breasts, leaving the imprint of her breasts, and bestowing grace. It is called Kamakoti. Now, what are called "kama" (desires) in this world are wife, children, gold, ornaments, houses, and many other riches. Because Kamakshi Devi grants crores of these desires, these riches, to those who come and worship her at the Kamakshi Devi Temple, that bilam is also called Kamakoti. Lord Shiva burned one Kama (god of love) with the fire from His third eye. When that burned Kama turned to ashes, Devi, playfully, looked at the burned Kama with the corner of her two eyes, that is, with a sidelong glance, and that one Kama was created as a crore Kamas by Devi. Therefore, that bilam where this Ambika resides is called Kamakoti. Furthermore, there are four purusharthas (aims of human life). Among the four types of benefits that souls attain, dharma, artha, kama, and moksha, kama comes third. The culmination of that kama, that is, the end of kama, is moksha. Because Ambika grants moksha, the culmination of kama, to those who take refuge in her, that bilam, that place where Kamakshi Devi, the Universal Mother, resides, is called Kamakoti. The golden luminous vimana where Kamakshi Devi resides is called Kamakoti Vimana. Furthermore, the single-syllable Pranava (Om) divides into three syllables: A, U, M. These three syllables represent the Trimurtis: Brahma, Vishnu, and Rudra, respectively. It is also called Kamakoti because she created these three deities a crore times from her eyes in every yuga. Ka means Saraswati, Ma means Lakshmi. Because Devi created Saraswati and Lakshmi from her two eyes, the Goddess was named Kamakshi. Furthermore, as stated in the Tiruvekamba Padalam in Kanchipuram, Ka means Brahma, A means Vishnu, M means Rudra. Because Ambika created Brahma (Ka), Vishnu (A), and Rudra (M) from her three eyes – the right eye, the left eye, and the third eye – she is named Kamakshi. The place where Kamakshi Ammaiyar resides is the Bindu Sthanam. Surrounding it is a beautiful chakra shape. The Bindu Sthanam in the center of the Sri Chakra is the place where Devi resides. That place is the bilam. That place is the bilakasha. It is the abode of all desired objects and is called Kamakottam. It is called Kamakoti, and in Tamil, it is called Kamakottam. It is our Saivite tradition to add "Tiru" before it and call it Tirukkamakottam. This bilam is the first among all other peedams, so it is called Adi Peedam. In such a bilam, in such a Kamakottam, in such a Kamakoti, Kamakshi Devi, who is a Sakti of Lord Shiva, resides blissfully as the cause of everything, as everything herself, and with one foot in the Nivartti Kala of the Prithvi Tattva, with two feet in the Pratishtha Kala of the Appu (water) Tattva, with four feet in the Vidya Kala of the Agni (fire), with eight feet in the Shanti Kala of the Vayu (air), with nine feet in the Shanti Atita Kala of the Akasha (space), and with a thousand feet in the transcendent space beyond that, the Parasakti resides. The Parasakti resides thus. That Parasakti, the mistress of all syllables, who is black, red, and white in color, who is in the form of a bilam in Kamakoti, who is the daughter of the mountain, and who, with Maya, gave birth to all worlds, our Kamakshi Devi, resides there. Thus, according to the boon received from Lord Shiva, Kamakshi Devi eternally performs Shiva Puja as penance in the bilam of Kamakoti in Kacchi Tirukkamakottam, centrally located in Kanchipuram. Furthermore, we will continue to contemplate the glories of Kamakshi Devi. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 26 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam Periyapuranam Part 234 Mummaiyal Ulaganda Sarukkam Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. We are contemplating about the Kamakshi Amman Temple, which is centrally located in Kanchipuram, also known as Kacchi Tirukkamakottam. In that temple where Kamakshi Ambika performs eternal penance, Mahavishnu and Mahalakshmi also reside. Mahavishnu resides there under the name Kalvar Perumal. Let us contemplate a little about that history. This history is found in the Veerattakasa Padalam of the Kanchi Puranam. Once, on Mount Kailash, Lord Shiva affectionately called Parvati Devi "Kali." However, Parasakti, because Lord Shiva called her Kali, desired to shed her dark complexion and attain a golden hue. She wished to be known as Gauri and requested this from Lord Shiva. Lord Shiva told Ambika, "Go to Kanchipuram and perform penance. Then you will shed your dark complexion and be known as Gauri." Ambika, likewise, came to Kanchipuram, shed her dark complexion, attained a golden hue, and was called Gauri. One day, in Vaikuntha, Lord Vishnu was narrating this divine story to Mahalakshmi. At that time, Mahalakshmi, with great eagerness, said to Lord Vishnu, "Like Gauri, you too should shed your dark blue complexion and attain a golden hue." Mahalakshmi said this to Vishnu. At that time, Mahavishnu became a little angry. Nevertheless, according to Mahalakshmi's wish, he told Mahalakshmi, "I will shed my dark blue body and attain a golden form, a coral-red body." He then disappeared from that place, reached Kanchinagaram, created a Chakra Tirtha opposite the place called Veerattakasa, bathed in that tirtha, performed a vow, and performed penance there as Pachai Vanna Perumal (Green-hued Lord). Every day, he worshipped Lord Shiva, Veerattakasa Peruman, enshrined in Veerattakasa, with 14,000 red lotus flowers. Pleased by this, Lord Shiva appeared before Lord Vishnu and blessed him so that his body would become coral-red. With such a coral-red body, Lord Vishnu still resides in Kanchipuram as Pavalavanna Perumal (Coral-hued Lord). With such a coral-red body, Lord Vishnu goes to Vaikuntha. There, he shows his coral-red body to Mahalakshmi and says, "Look at this form." At that time, Mahalakshmi said, "Lord, you have attained this coral-red hue. Since you have attained this by your own will, it will vanish like magic and will not be permanent. What benefit is this to me?" Hearing this, Lord Vishnu became angry and cursed Mahalakshmi, saying, "Since you have scorned me, you will attain a dark complexion." At that time, the golden body of the Goddess of Wealth turned dark. Mahalakshmi expressed her sorrow, fell at Lord Vishnu's feet, and pleaded with him to remove the mistake she had made and avert the curse. Lord Vishnu then softened his heart and told Mahalakshmi, "Even if you are dark, we will be together as before. Do not fear." Nevertheless, Mahalakshmi wished to regain her former golden body. After consulting with her friends, she decided that if she went to Kanchipuram and performed penance before Ambika at the place where Kamakshi Devi resides on the banks of the Ulagaani Tirtha in the center of Kanchipuram, her golden hue would return. Without informing Mahavishnu, she went alone to Kanchipuram and performed penance before Kamakshi Devi. Mahalakshmi, performing penance before Kamakshi Ambika, requested her to restore her golden body. Mahalakshmi said to Kamakshi Devi, "Devi, you previously protected Saraswati, whose name is 'Ka,' like your own eye. Now, you should also protect me, whose name is 'Ma,' like your own eye, and thus add another ornament to the explanation of your divine name Kamakshi." Thus, as the Goddess of Wealth, who resides in the red lotus flower, praised her, Kamakshi Devi, the Goddess, out of compassion, appeared before the Goddess of Wealth and said, "O daughter of the lotus, do not tremble. The dark color of your body, that dark sandal paste, will be removed. You will attain a more beautiful form than before. Even now, you are the beloved wife of Lord Vishnu. For those who take the dark sandal paste that will be removed from your supremely beautiful body and apply it as a tilak on their forehead, marriage, happiness, wealth, and fame will increase manifold. Sins and evils will not approach them." Kamakshi Devi granted this boon. Saraswati, who has read and understood all the ancient Vedas and attained greatness, also resides here. "Now you too reside here, and as you wish, another explanation for the name Kamakshi will arise," thus Kamakshi Ambika showered grace upon both the Goddess of Wealth and the Goddess of Learning. While this conversation was happening between Kamakshi Devi and Mahalakshmi, at the same time, in Vaikuntha, in the inner chambers, Lord Vishnu, distressed by the absence of his wife Mahalakshmi, searched everywhere and finally came to Kanchinagaram and reached Kacchi Tirukkamakottam. He stood hidden and listened to Kamakshi Devi and Mahalakshmi talking. The Goddess Kamakshi, realizing that Mahavishnu was hiding, asked, "Who is hiding there?" At that time, the hidden Mayavan (Lord Vishnu) revealed himself, and Mayavan and the Goddess of Wealth together prostrated at the feet of the Goddess and, with joyful hearts, stood aside. Then Kamakshi Ambika said to Mahavishnu, "Mayavan, this is my charitable institution where 32 acts of dharma are fostered. A powerful great bhuta (demon) is roaming here, obstructing these acts of dharma. You must subdue its wickedness." Lord Vishnu, bowing to Ambika, subdued the bhuta that was roaming on the banks of the Pancha Tirtha and resided there. Lord Vishnu then came and worshipped Kamakshi Devi. At that time, Kamakshi Devi commanded Mahalakshmi to be on one side and Lord Vishnu on the other side of her abode, the bilakasha. Because Lord Vishnu had hidden like a thief and listened to Kamakshi Devi and Mahalakshmi talking, he acquired the name Kalvar Perumal (Thief Lord) in the Kamakshi Amman Temple. On either side of Kamakshi Devi's bilakasha, Mahalakshmi resides as Arupa Lakshmi (formless Lakshmi) on one side, and Mahavishnu resides as Kalvar Perumal on the other side. The place where Mahavishnu resides here is one of the 108 Divya Desams praised by the Alvars, known as Tirukkalvanur, and the Perumal there is known by the name Kalvar Perumal. He has been praised and mangalasasanam (auspiciously sung) by Tirumangai Alvar, one of the 12 Alvars, as "Kalva." In Kanchipuram, inside the Ekambaranathar Temple, Mahavishnu is enshrined under the name Nilattundu Tingal Perumal. We have already seen that this is also a Divya Desam. Similarly, Kalvar Perumal inside the Kamakshi Amman Temple is also enshrined as one of the 108 Divya Desams. All these are details found in the Kanchi Puranam. Now, Sekkizhar Peruman continues to sing about Kacchi Tirukkamakottam. Sekkizhar Peruman refers to Kamakshi Ambika, who resides in Tirukkamakottam in Kanchipuram, by the divine name "Aram Perum Selvi" (Goddess of Great Dharma).

Appar Peruman sings, "Aram Perum Selvi" (Goddess of Great Dharma) as one of the divine names of Kamakshi Devi, which is also mentioned in lexicons. There is a street in Kanchipuram named "Aram Perum Selvi Teru," which is now known as Arappananjeri Teru. There is a miraculous plant in the Kanchipuram temple. This plant has a single stem that produces three blue flowers. It is a wonder that it blooms three blue flowers on a single stem once a year. Parasakti, whose home is the entire universe with all its clusters of worlds, lights three lamps in her home: the sun, the moon, and fire. But now she resides eternally in Kanchipuram. Therefore, the sun, moon, and fire, the three lamps she lit in her old home, the universe, have come to Kanchipuram as if to assist the Mother, blooming as three blue flowers on a single stem of a single plant. Sekkizhar Peruman describes this in his verses. Now Sekkizhar Peruman sings about the Ulagaani Tirtha inside the Kamakshi Amman Temple in Kanchipuram. "The Tirukkamakottam, praised for its excellent tirthas that remove evil, has a beautiful, cool, deep pool of water, the Ulagaani, which is revered by the inhabitants of all three worlds. It is like a ladder that helps those living on this earth attain the higher state of liberation, and it has always existed as a lofty form." This is a verse composed by Sekkizhar Peruman. There is a tirtha next to the Tirukkamakottam, the Kamakshi Amman Temple, in Kanchipuram. It is a cool-water pond. It is a tirtha praised and worshipped by the inhabitants of the upper, middle, and lower worlds. For those living on this earth, it is like a ladder with steps designed to help them attain the higher state of liberation. That tirtha exists. Because it acts as a ladder, elevating those who bathe in it from this world to the world of liberation, and because it is praised and worshipped by people from various worlds, it is called Ulagaani Tirtha. Generally, sacred tirthas are those that remove the sins of those who bathe in them and absorb those sins themselves. However, this Ulagaani Tirtha has the greatness that all those sacred tirthas come and bathe in it to cleanse themselves of the sins they have absorbed. When narrating the history of the Mahamaham Tirtha in Tirukkudamookku (Kumbakonam), it is said that all other tirthas in India absorbed the sins of those who bathed in them. Appar Peruman sings in Tiruttandagam that all those other tirthas then come to the Mahamaham Tirtha to cleanse themselves of the sins they have absorbed. "Tavi mudal Kaveri nal Yamunai Gangai Saraswati Potramarai Porkarani tennir Koviyodu Kumari varu tirttam suzhnda Kudantai Kizhkottattu em Koottanare" is a Tiruttandagam by Appar Swamigal. This Ulagaani Tirtha is in the form of a downward-facing well. Nevertheless, it also possesses the quality of a ladder, a means to elevate souls to the world of liberation. We can recall the ancient inscriptions that speak of "upward-facing trees and downward-facing wells." Some also say that this tirtha is the very form of the Mother. For this reason, the truth that the five elements are included in Shiva's Ashtamurtis and possess the nature of the Mother's form is mentioned in the Tiruppadigam sung by Aludaiya Pillaiyar in Tirualavay.

Ulagaani is the name of the tirtha, and it is also mentioned in the Kanchi Puranam that the Mother performed penance and established a charitable institution on its banks. Such is the glory of that Ulagaani Tirtha, located within the Kamakshi Amman Temple in Kanchipuram. Furthermore, Sekkizhar Peruman sings about a wonder called "Disai Mayakkam" (Directional Delusion). In Tirukkamakottam in Kanchipuram, the sun and moon, in the sky directly above it, deviate from their usual paths and move to one side. As a result, the directions are altered, and the shadows also change in that place. Sekkizhar Peruman sings that this wonder is visible to all people in this world every day and is still present in this era. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 27 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam Periyapuranam Part 235 Mummaiyal Ulaganda Sarukkam Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. Next, Sekkizhar sings about a place called Kanchidanam. He sings that in Kanchipuram, on the banks of a river named Kanchi, there is a temple called Tirupperum Peyar Irukkai, where Lord Shiva lovingly resides. Tirupperum Peyar Irukkai is the name of a temple in Kanchipuram. "Perum Peyar" means "Mahavakyam" (Great Utterance). It is the place where Lord Shiva, the God spoken of by the Mahavakyams mentioned in the Vedas, resides, hence the name Tirupperum Peyar Irukkai. "Perum Peyar Kadavulil Kandu" is the preface to Sivagnana Bodham. "Perum Peyar Iyavul" is a phrase from Tirumurugatruppadai. Now, he calls that place Kanchidanam. A place with Kanchi trees becomes Kanchidanam. The special feature of the Kanchi tree is that its shadow does not leave its base but remains confined there when the sun rises and when it sets in the western sea. Such a place where Kanchi trees are found is Kanchidanam. Furthermore, in this verse, Kanchipuram is sung as "Kanni Nannedum Kaappu." Kanchipuram also has the name "Kannikkaappu." Among the 12 names of Kanchipuram, one is Kannikkaappu. This is because Goddess Durga guards Kanchipuram, hence the name Kannikkaappu. Its history is as follows: Once, when Lord Shiva and Uma Ammaiyar were seated on Mount Mandara, the Lord affectionately called the Goddess "Kali." This was because Ambika had a dark complexion, wasn't she? So, he called her Kali. Hearing the Lord call her Kali, the Goddess became distressed, shed tears like pearls, and said, "My Lord, you call me this because I have a dark complexion. You, however, have a pure crystal complexion. How can we be compatible? I now wish to shed this dark complexion and attain a golden complexion, to become Gauri. You must grant me this grace." The Lord then blessed her, saying, "Go to Kanchinagaram, worship me, perform flawless penance, and you will attain the golden complexion you desire and be known as Gauri." So, Parvati Devi took leave of the Lord, went to Kanchinagaram, performed great penance on the banks of the Pancha Tirtha, and attained a golden complexion, becoming Gauri. Because she was called Kali, the Goddess worshipped with that dark complexion. Lord Shiva then blessed the Devi, saying, "The dark-colored sheath from your body will be removed, and you will attain a golden complexion, known as Gauri." From that dark-colored sheath removed from the Goddess's body, the immensely powerful Durga emerged. As soon as the dark sheath left the Goddess's body, the Goddess attained a golden complexion. Durga, who emerged from the Goddess's dark complexion, was called Kausiki. Kausiki performed great devotion, consecrated a Shiva Lingam, and worshipped it. Therefore, that Lingam came to be known as Kausikisam. Kausiki, that Durga, prayed to Lord Shiva, and the Lord told Durga, "Durga, protect Kanchinagaram and reside here." Thus, he blessed her. Because Durga guards it, Kanchipuram acquired the name Kannikkaappu. That Kausiki, guarding Kanchinagaram as we mentioned before, consecrated the Kausiki Lingam and, with great devotion, worshipped it according to Agamic rites, living happily herself and ensuring that all beings lived without any suffering. At that time, seeing the asuras Sumban and Nisumban causing distress to all beings, Lord Brahma came to Kanchinagaram and pleaded with the Lord. Then, by the command of Lord Shiva, that Durga, Kausiki, went to the Vindhya Mountains, resided there, destroyed Sumban and Nisumban, and returned to Kanchinagaram, continuing to guard Kanchipuram as before and living happily. That is why Kanchipuram also acquired the name Kannikkaappu. Now, in the next verse, Sekkizhar Peruman praises the various Shiva temples in Kanchipuram, which is called Kacchi Palatali. He sings that in many places within the city limits of Kanchipuram, Lord Shiva resides, pleased, to bestow grace upon the sages who, through Vedic mantras, great penances, and strict observances, perform worship in an orderly manner. Similarly, in many places within the city limits of Kanchipuram, Lord Shiva resides, pleased, fulfilling the desires of all beings, including the Devas, who come to Kanchipuram and worship Him with abundant love. We have already seen various temples within Kanchipuram as part of the essence of the Kanchi Puranam. Sekkizhar Peruman compiles all such temples in this verse. Kacchi Tiruneri, Karaikkadu, Punniyakotteesam, Sivasthanam, Manikkandeesam, Sarndasayam, Saptasthanam, Parasareesam, Adipideesam, Mukteesam, Panadareesam, Kayaroganam, Sittisam, Arisapa Payam Tirttasthanam, Ishtasittisam, Kacchapesam, Sahodara Tirtha, Surahareesam, Tandondreesam, Amaresam, Anekathangavatam, Kailayam, Veeraragavesam, Palabhadra Ramesam, Bhairavesam, Visvasenaisam, Takkesam, Muppurarikkottam, Hiranyesam, Narasimkesam, Andhakesam, Vanesam, Tiruvonakaantan Tali, Jalandhareesam, Tirumalperu, Parasuramesam, Renukesam, Yogacharyar Tali, Sarva Tirtha, Navagrahesam, Piravasthanam, Iravasthanam, Mahalingam, Veerattakasa, Pandavesam, Macchesam, Abhiramesam, Kannesam, Masattan Tali, Ananthapadmanabhesam, Kacchi Mayanam, and many other such places, and still other places, are abundant everywhere in Kanchipuram. "Kacchi Palataliyum Ekambattum Kailaya Nathanaiye Kaanalame" is a Kshetra Kovai Tiruttandagam. Now, next, Sekkizhar Peruman sings about two places called Iravasthanam (place of no death) and Piravasthanam (place of no birth). He also sings that there is a place in Kanchi where a rabbit chases an elephant, which is a matter of great pride. Furthermore, the two places, Iravasthanam and Piravasthanam, sung by Sekkizhar Peruman, are mentioned in the Kanchi Puranam. First, the place called Piravasthanam. In Kanchipuram, in ancient times, in the Indrachala Prapancham, a great sage named Vamadeva, while still in his mother's womb, was distressed by the suffering of birth. Fearing this birth, which even great sages are afraid of, and with a distressed mind, he placed Lord Shiva, the beginningless and endless great light of bliss, in the lotus of his heart and prayed. "O Mahadeva, may I not be born again in this illusory universe, bewildered by countless sufferings, withered, tormented, dying, and again and again revolving in the ocean of birth and death, lost, floundering, confused, and disappearing without knowing where I was born, where I grew up, and where I died," thus the sage Vamadeva prayed with great love.

Immediately, the Lord appeared and told the sage, "Do not fear. Be born on earth, go to Kanchinagaram, and worship me. If you do so, the terrible obstacle of birth will not approach you." The Lord thus blessed him. The sage then came to Kanchipuram, consecrated a Shiva Lingam, worshipped it according to Agamic rites, and as he wished, was freed from the suffering of birth and lived as a Chiranjeevi (immortal). Therefore, that place is called Piravasthanam. The Kanchi Puranam states that those who come and worship at this place called Piravasthanam in Kanchipuram will not be born again in a mother's womb and suffer. Furthermore, they will receive the grace of Lord Paramashivan and live a glorious life. Vamadeva Muni, after worshipping at Piravasthanam and receiving the boon of no rebirth, went to Kailashagiri, bowed at the lotus feet of the Lord, and, taking the Lingam given to him, returned to Kanchinagaram. He then installed the Shiva Lingam with the divine name Mukteesan next to Gnana Tirtha, west of Piravasthanam, and lived happily, worshipping it. Next is the place called Iravasthanam. Next to this Piravasthanam is the place called Iravasthanam, where Lord Shiva resides. "Puli adikkum mun kili adikkum" (The parrot strikes before the tiger strikes) is a proverb. Its meaning is that the fear of death comes even before death arrives. Sages who feared death were performing great penance. At that time, Lord Brahma came and advised the sages, "If you wish to remove your fear of death, go to Kanchipuram and worship Lord Shiva." All of them came to Kanchinagaram, consecrated Shiva Lingams, and lived happily, having received the boon of immortality. Therefore, that place is called Iravasthanam. Svetan also came here, worshipped Lord Shiva, and protected himself from the agony of death. Markandeya Muni also praised and worshipped the Lord at this place, defied Yama, and lived as a Chiranjeevi. The grandson of Salankayana also worshipped the Lord at this place, did not die, and became a leader of Shiva's ganas. Many others, when their lifespan was about to end, came here, lovingly praised the Lord, and received the boon of immortality. Therefore, the Kanchi Puranam states that those who wish for longevity should come and worship at this place called Iravasthanam. Now, Sekkizhar Peruman sings about the tirthas in Kanchipuram. There is a tirtha in Kanchipuram called Punniya Tirtha. It is also called Turita Nasa Tirtha. This Punniya Tirtha removes all evil deeds and bestows happiness. Next is a tirtha called Ishta Siddhi Tirtha. It is located in Kacchapesam. Bathing in it on a Sunday is special. As its name suggests, this Ishta Siddhi Tirtha grants the desires of those who come and bathe in it. Its name is also mentioned in Silappathikaram. Next is Nanmangala Tirtha. Although there is a grammatical usage that refers to inauspicious things as auspicious and things that do not bring great auspiciousness as auspicious, this tirtha is called Mangala Tirtha because it brings great benefits. Bathing in it on a Tuesday is very special. Next is Kappudai Tirtha. It is also called Surarakshitam. "Kappu" here refers to the protection of the Devas. Next is Sarva Tirtha. Bathing in it on a Saturday is very special. "Sarva" means that all tirthas from all three worlds are present here. It is located at the chariot stand of the Tiruvekambam Temple. Besides these tirthas, there are many other tirthas. It is very regrettable that these tirthas, which should be in a state where humans can bathe, have now been brought to a deplorable state by people. It is a great act of Shiva Punya for devotees to maintain these pure tirthas in a clean state and celebrate them. Having sung all this, Sekkizhar Peruman now sings about the wonders (vimmithangal) in Kanchipuram. Vimmithangal means wonders.

Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 28 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam Periyapuranam Part 236. Mummaiyal Ulaganda Sarukkam Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. Sekkizhar Peruman sings about the wonders (vimmithangal) located in Kanchipuram. Vimmithangal means wonders or special features. As we have already seen, in the Kamakshi Amman Temple in Kanchipuram, there is a pond with golden lotuses where three flowers bloom on a single stem. The Kampa River flows there. The Kampa River is a river that flows underground. Even now, it is noteworthy that water flows beneath the wells in Kanchipuram. Next, in Kanchipuram, the water lily (Neelotpal malar), which blooms at night, blooms during the day. Accordingly, the lotus, which blooms during the day, blooms at night. Similarly, the Paatalam flower, which blooms in the evening, blooms at noon. There is a tamarind tree called Kanpadatha Puli (unseen tamarind). The leaves of this tree do not close at night. Now, there is a tamarind tree called Kaya Puli (unripe tamarind). This tamarind ripens directly without first forming unripe fruit. Similarly, near Coimbatore, in Tirupperur, there is a tamarind tree called Pirava Puli (unborn tamarind). Its seeds, if sown in the ground, do not sprout, as mentioned in the Perur Puranam. Similarly, in the Kanchi Puranam, there is a separate chapter called Vimmitha Padalam. In it, the author of the Kanchi Puranam describes all the special features and wonders of Kanchipuram. There are many Shiva Lingams in the city of Kanchipuram. There are Lingams that turn everything they touch into gold. There are Lingams worshipped by countless Siddhas. There are Shiva Lingams that quickly grant desired objects. There are Shiva Lingams that can only be seen by those with penance. The wonder of one supreme being becoming two, and those two being non-different and merging, also exists in Kanchinagaram. What is that wonder? It is that the one supreme being, Shiva, becomes two entities, Shiva and Shakti, that is, in Kanchinagaram, Ekambaranathar and Kamakshi, and then they merge again to become one. Kanchipuram is therefore called the place where one entity becomes many and many entities become one. Lord Shiva, who is one, resides there as many. Many Vedas exist there as a single great mango tree. Furthermore, Kanchipuram boasts three wondrous sthanas (places), three wondrous peedams (seats), three wondrous kalanis (fields), four wondrous simhasanas (thrones), five wondrous virukshas (trees), five wondrous tadagas (ponds), six wondrous pakshis (birds), seven wondrous podu sthanas (public places), eight wondrous poom poigaigal (flower ponds), nine wondrous kallugal (stones), nine wondrous pooncholais (flower gardens), ten wondrous sabhas (assemblies), and eleven wondrous kinatrugal (wells). All this is mentioned in the Kanchi Puranam. Their explanations are also given there. First, we saw that there are three wondrous sthanas, didn't we? What are these three wondrous sthanas? One wondrous sthana shows us the fathers we had in our many previous births. The second wondrous sthana shows us the mothers who gave birth to us in many previous births. The third wondrous sthana shows us those who were our relatives in many previous births. Now, next are the three wondrous peedams. The first wondrous peedam provides food and water to those who come to it. Another wondrous peedam quickly grants all desired objects. The third wondrous peedam makes the mute speak and the speaker mute. Next are the three wondrous kalanis (fields). One wondrous kalani produces rice grains again and again, no matter how many times they are harvested. Another wondrous kalani yields fruit on the very day the seeds are sown. Another wondrous kalani produces good crops without needing to be plowed with a plow. Next are the four wondrous simhasanas (thrones). The first wondrous simhasana shows Lord Brahma. Just as Lord Shiva has three empowered vyuhas (manifestations) – Brahma, Vishnu, and Rudra – Lord Brahma, Lord Vishnu, and Lord Rudra also have three vyuhas each. On this first simhasana, Lord Brahma is shown seated. Lord Brahma will be seated there with his three vyuhas: Virat, Kalan, and Purushan. On the second simhasana, Lord Vishnu will be seated, being praised by his vyuhas: Sankarshanan, Pradyumnan, and Aniruddhan. On the third simhasana, Kalagni Rudra will be seated, surrounded by his three vyuhas: Aran, Mrudan, and Bhavan. On the fourth wondrous simhasana, Lord Surya will be seated with his associated deities. Furthermore, in Kanchipuram, there is a golden-flowered Sutha Viruksham (mango tree), and a Kanchi Viruksham (Kanchi tree) that continuously drips sweet honey and keeps its shadow to itself, not offering it to others. There is a tamarind tree called Uranga Puli (sleepless tamarind) that yields fruits directly without flowering or bearing unripe fruit. The Iruppai tree continuously produces various kinds of flowers. The Athi tree continuously produces golden flowers. These five divine trees grow wonderfully and majestically in Kanchi Nagaram, where Lord Shiva, the God of gods, resides in glory. These are the five wondrous trees mentioned earlier. Now, the five wondrous tadagas (ponds). In one wondrous tadaga, lotuses bloom at night and water lilies bloom during the day. In another wondrous tadaga, three lotus flowers bloom on a single stem. Another wondrous tadaga quickly turns those who bathe in it into monkeys. Another wondrous tadaga brings trouble to the enemies of those who bathe in it. Another wondrous tadaga brings happiness to the relatives of those who bathe in it. These are the five wondrous tadagas. Now, the six wondrous pakshis (birds). First, the Mayura Pakshi (peacock) calls out even during droughts, bringing rain. The second bird, the Kilippillai (parrot), explains all the Vedas and Agamic scriptures clearly. The third bird, the Aandai (owl), teaches all mathematical texts flawlessly. Next is the Kabota Pakshi (pigeon), which knows all astrological scriptures and tells them to others. The next bird is the Naganaavay Pakshi, which sings according to the principles of music. Sixth is the Chakravaaka Pakshi, which grants all desired objects. Thus, these six birds exist in Kanchipuram. Now, the seven wondrous podu sthanas (public places). In the first podu sthana, the Pancha Vadyams (five musical instruments) continuously play without ceasing. Similarly, there are seven wondrous sthanas in Kanchipuram. Now, the eight wondrous poom poigaigal (flower ponds). One pond gives a golden complexion to those who bathe in it. One pond gives a ruby complexion. One pond grants the pleasures of Devendra. One pond grants all desired objects. One pond grants everything desired. One pond grants true knowledge desired by the great. One pond grants the eternal abode of truth and knowledge. One pond removes all karmic diseases. Thus, there are eight wondrous flower ponds in Kanchipuram that destroy the impurities of ego and others. Now, the nine kallugal (stones). One stone turns those who touch it into Devas. One stone removes snakebite poison. One stone grants the Ashtama Siddhis (eight great powers) like Anima. One stone brings back a soul that has left the body. One stone stands impartially and resolves disputes, bringing justice to plaintiffs and defendants. One stone removes terrible diseases like leprosy. One stone reattaches severed limbs to the body as before. One stone removes great sins that relentlessly follow through many births. One stone grants supreme knowledge. Thus, there are nine wondrous stones in Kanchipuram. Now, the nine wondrous pooncholais (flower gardens). In one flower garden, pearls rain. In one flower garden, rubies rain. In one flower garden, diamonds rain, and in another,

In one, vaidurya (cat's eye) rains, in another, pushparaga (topaz) rains, in another, garudapachai (emerald) rains, in another, indranila (sapphire) rains. Thus, in all nine flower gardens, navaratnas (nine gems) rain. Next are the 10 wondrous sabhas (assemblies). One sabha makes those who have sinned and come to it mute. One sabha makes thieves lose their way and become confused. One sabha clearly teaches knowledge. One sabha grants various forms. One sabha grants immortality. One sabha shows the way to the netherworld. One sabha causes even the clever to become bewildered. One sabha rains rubies. One sabha shows day as night and night as day. One sabha makes those who come to it invisible to others, causing astonishment. Thus, there are 10 wondrous sabhas in Kanchipuram. Now, there are 11 wondrous wells in Kanchipuram. They are the curd well, milk well, ghee well, honey well, sugarcane juice well, path to liberation well, path to heaven well, worldly conduct well, a well that miraculously hides the shadow of those who peer into it, a well that produces water without seeds, and a well that makes those who came quickly go back. Thus, there are 11 wondrous wells there. Not only that, but there are many other wonders. There is an oil press there. If sesame seeds are put into it, oil appears on the other side. There is a stone and a stone idol that make everyone experience the consequences of their own actions. The anklets of Bhairava, the guardian deity of Kanchinagaram, continuously jingle "kalil kalil" as he dances, astounding everyone. Kanchipuram is boundless with such wonders. No one knows all these wonders. These wonders will be visible to everyone in the Krita Yuga, Treta Yuga, and Dvapara Yuga. In the last yuga, the Kali Yuga, these wonders will be visible only to some ascetics. They will not be visible to others. Just as Lord Shiva, by His grace, has created many wonders in Shivaloka for those who attain liberation to see and enjoy, these wonders have been created by Lord Shiva for those living in Kanchinagaram to see and rejoice. Thus, based on Sekkizhar's verses about the wonders of Kanchipuram, we have seen the wonders of Kanchipuram mentioned in a chapter called Vimmitha Padalam in the Kanchi Puranam. Now, Sekkizhar Peruman sings that Lord Ganesha, Lord Bhairava, and Lord Muruga guard Kanchipuram. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 29 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam Periyapuranam Part 237 Mummaiyal Ulaganda Sarukkam Tiruk Kuripputtonta Nayanar Puranam. Sekkizhar Peruman next sings that Lord Ganesha, Lord Bhairava, and Lord Muruga guard Kanchipuram. Lord Ganesha, Bhairavamurthy, and Lord Muruga are revered as the sons of Shiva. History states that all three of them guard Kanchipuram. Therefore, Sekkizhar Peruman describes in this verse how these three guard it. First, Sekkizhar Peruman sings about Lord Ganesha. Lord Ganesha has five large hands. Lord Ganesha is the peerless elephant-faced deity who has ichor flowing like a river. He guards Kanchipuram. In this verse, Sekkizhar Peruman refers to Lord Ganesha as "Anjuvan Karam Aaru Ezhi Madham Or Aanai" (One elephant with five hands and six flowing ichors). First, the explanation of "Anjuvan Karam." It is our tradition to praise Lord Ganesha as having five hands, as in "Ainthu Karathanai Aanai Mugathanai." The number five here has been corrupted to "Anju" in the verse. "Vaan" here should be understood as "for the celestials." "Anjuvan" means "celestials who fear," implying that Lord Ganesha protected the Devas who feared the suffering caused by Gajamukhasura, and also that Gajamukhasura, whom even the Devas feared, feared Lord Ganesha. Lord Ganesha protects with his hands. With one tusk, which he held in one of his divine hands, he used that single tusk as a weapon to destroy the cruel Gajamukhasura. Therefore, this act of protection is described here as belonging to Lord Ganesha's divine hand. Because he took one of his tusks and used it as a weapon for protection, Lord Ganesha acquired the names Ottraikkomban (one-tusked) and Orukottan (single-tusked). Based on this idea, Arunanandi Shivacharyar Swamigal begins his Gnana Nool (book of wisdom), Sivagnana Siddhiyar, by praising Lord Ganesha as Orukottan in the invocation verse. We saw that Lord Ganesha has five hands. These five divine hands of Lord Ganesha hold a noose, an ankus (elephant goad), a jewel pot, a tusk, and a modakam (sweet dumpling). Next, Sekkizhar Peruman refers to Lord Ganesha as "Aaru Ezhi Madham." Lord Ganesha has two types of ichor flowing like a river: Kannamadham (ichor from the temples) and Kabolamadham (ichor from the cheeks). Since only his face is that of an elephant, Kannamadham and Kabolamadham are naturally attributed to him. However, it is our customary usage to refer to him as Mummadhathan (one with three ichors). His appearance as an elephant for the sake of Lord Muruga was due to an urgent situation. Such is Lord Ganesha, the elephant-faced deity. The Ganesha Murthy mentioned by Sekkizhar Peruman in this verse is Vikatachakra Vinayaka Murthy, who is enshrined in the thousand-pillared mandapam at the southern entrance of Tiruvekambam. In the Kanchi Puranam, the author of the Kanchi Puranam has composed a hymn to Lord Ganesha as a hymn to Vikatachakra Vinayaka. In it, he has sung the history of why this Ganesha is named Vikatachakra Vinayaka. Lord Vishnu obtained the Chakra Ayudha (discus weapon) from Lord Shiva by plucking his own lotus-like eye and offering it in worship. Lord Vishnu also participated in Daksha's yagna. At that time, when Veerapathira Swamy came to destroy Daksha's yagna, Vishnu hurled his Chakra Ayudha at Veerapathira Swamy. A skull from the garland of skulls worn by Veerapathira Swamy swallowed the hurled Chakra Ayudha. At that time, Vishvaksenar, the commander of Lord Vishnu's army, arrived and performed a dance, a kuttu. Seeing this, the skull that had swallowed the Chakra Ayudha laughed. At that moment, the Chakra Ayudha came out again. Lord Ganesha then took possession of that Chakra Ayudha. This was because, once before, the Chakra Ayudha had been defiled by the blood that came from the body of Jalandharasura. Therefore, Lord Ganesha now took the Chakra Ayudha with the intention of removing its defilement. So, Lord Ganesha now took possession of the Chakra Ayudha. At this time, Vishvaksenar again came before Ganesha and performed a Konangi Kuttu, a comical dance. Accepting that comical dance as worship, Lord Ganesha removed the defilement of the Chakra Ayudha and returned it to Vishvaksenar. Therefore, the Ganesha enshrined in the Ekambaranathar Temple in Kanchipuram acquired the divine name Vikatachakra Vinayaka. Sekkizhar Peruman has sung about this Vikatachakra Vinayaka Peruman here. The divine incarnation of Lord Ganesha is sung by Madhava Sivagnana Swamigal in the Valamburi Vinayaka Padalam of the Kanchi Puranam. One day, on Mount Mandara, Lord Shiva entered a Chitramandapam (picture hall) with Parvati Devi. There were various wonderful paintings there. Lord Shiva pointed out to Parvati Devi what each of these was, along with great mantras and sub-mantras. At that time, Parvati Devi looked at the two wonderfully prominent Pranava mantras at the entrance of the mandapam many times and was greatly pleased. She then requested Lord Shiva, saying, "My Lord, please explain to me what these two, which are so wonderfully prominent among the many paintings depicted here, are." Lord Shiva then said, "These two are called Pranava mantras. This one belongs to me. This Pranava is the source of the Trimurtis. That one belongs to you. That is the root cause of the three Shaktis. Since these Pranava mantras, the mother and father of all Vedas and Agamas, appear before us as a male elephant and a female elephant, let us both assume those forms and dance." Saying this, Lord Shiva took the form of a male elephant, and Parvati Devi took the form of a female elephant. They then looked with their gracious gaze at the two Pranavas, Samashti and Vyashti Pranavas, present there. At that time, Lord Ganesha, with an elephant face, was born so that all the worlds surrounded by the ocean might be saved. Seeing this, both of them, with great love, took Lord Ganesha, placed him on their laps, embraced him, and blessed him. Then Parvati Devi bowed at the feet of Lord Shiva and, with overflowing love and sweet words, requested, "My Lord, please bestow the title of Ganapathi upon this son whom you have lovingly given, so that the ganas, ganadhipas, and everyone else may praise him." Lord Shiva granted that boon according to the wish of the Goddess. All the Devas, Munis, Garudas, Gandharvas, and other ganas came together. Lord Shiva then pointed to the small great deity on his lap, saying, "This boy is very dear to Uma Devi, the daughter of the mountain. He is our eldest son. Bring the necessary implements to crown him as the lord of all worlds." After they brought everything, he seated the son on the throne and performed the coronation. He then advised Brahma, Vishnu, Devendra, and other Devas, saying, "He is the Lord who will remove all obstacles and protect you. He will create obstacles for the actions of asuras and destroy them." All the Devas then worshipped and praised Ganesha, Lord Ganesha. Thus, the Saiva Maha Puranas and the Kanchi Puranam describe the divine incarnation of Lord Ganesha. Such a Lord Ganesha resides in the Ekambaranathar Temple in Kanchipuram as Vikatachakra Vinayaka Murthy and guards Kanchipuram. Not only that, but in Kanchipuram, in the Kshetra called Anekatangavatam, Lord Ganesha consecrates and worships a Shiva Lingam. Lord Ganesha is the son of Shiva, with legs like a mortar, small eyes, a swift gait, flowing ichor, a tusk like a crescent moon, great strength, and a trumpeting sound. He came to Kanchinagaram, and with great love, intending to worship Lord Shiva, he consecrated a Lingam named Anekapeesan in his own name and eagerly worshipped it according to Agamic rites. At that time, Lord Shiva appeared before him and asked, "My son, what boon do you desire?" Lord Ganesha then fell at the lotus feet of his father and requested, "Father, what is the task I should perform in this world? Please command me." Lord Shiva then said to Lord Ganesha, "My son, you must uphold the principles of the Vedic scriptures, ensure that the good deeds undertaken by virtuous people are completed without obstacles, and create obstacles for asuras. Furthermore, you must always create obstacles for those who spread false doctrines of external religions. You must remove suffering and provide support to those who adhere to the path of Vedic and Agamic Saiva Siddhanta, so that no obstacles arise for them. Therefore, everyone will praise you. Even if they are highly esteemed Vedic scholars, the actions of those who do not worship you will be fruitless on earth. All who bow at your feet and praise you will attain great wealth. Auspicious deeds will become inauspicious for those who do not worship and praise you. O great one, I have granted you all these boons. Now listen to one more thing.
